\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ (g.t_{\texttt{Nat}})\;|\;(\ell,\ell^{\prime},g,U_{S},U_{T},x_{S},x_{T}.t_{\Pi})\;|\;(\ell,g.t_{\texttt{Ty}})\;|\;(\ell,g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\texttt{El}})

(Branches for code of terms)

\displaystyle b_{\textsf{Trm}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ (\ell,g,U_{T},u_{x}.t_{x})\;|\;(g.t^{\prime}_{\texttt{Nat}})\;|\;(\ell,\ell^{\prime},g,u_{s},u_{t},x_{s},x_{t}.t^{\prime}_{\Pi})\;|\;(\ell,g.t^{\prime}_{\texttt{Ty}})

\displaystyle\;|\;(g.t_{\textsf{zero}})\;|\;(g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\textsf{succ}})\;|\;(\ell,g,U_{M},u_{s},u_{s^{\prime}},u_{t},x_{M},x_{s},x_{s^{\prime}},x_{t}.t_{\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}})

\displaystyle\;|\;(\ell,\ell^{\prime},g,U_{S},U_{T},u_{t},x_{S},x_{t}.t_{\lambda})\;|\;(\ell,\ell^{\prime},g,U_{S},U_{T},u_{t},u_{s},x_{S},x_{T},x_{t},x_{s}.t_{\textsf{app}})

Following the layering principle before, we index our judgments with an layer index

i
. We include four layers and these layers are summarized in
Table

1
. To elaborate, we begin with layer
v
, which is the layer that contains only
variables. This layer is needed to describe the base case of the recursive principles for code
when a local variable is hit. Layer
v
(for variables) is
not
available for most rules given in

Sec.

4.4
other than the local variable rule and its congruence. We will follow this convention for the rest of this report, unless we specifically
state that layer
v
is available for particular rules. Layer
c
(for code) is the layer for code of MLTT. This layer is akin to layer
0
in
Sec.

2
and
3
, where static code
resides and no computation is allowed. However, in order to capture code promotions, we must introduce another layer,
d
(for
programs), to permit computation of MLTT
programs
in local contexts and on the
type level. This layer is especially crucial in the recursive principle for terms for the argument

T
where code promotions are implicitly handled by definitional equivalence. However, no meta-programs are allowed at layer
d
; in other words, the language at
layer
d
is virtually vanilla MLTT. Therefore, unlike simple types, there are two layers in
DeLaM
permitting
computation. At last, we have layer
m
(for meta-programs) where we have the power to do
meta-programming. At this layer, we have access to not only universe-polymorphic functions, but also
contextual types and recursive principles for code. All meta-functions must reside at this layer. All layers are related by a strict order of
\textsc{v}<\textsc{c}<\textsc{d}<\textsc{m}
.

The reason to introduce layer
d
becomes obvious by considering which layer the type
of a given MLTT term should live in. For instance, given a judgment
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, we know
t
lives at layer
c
as
code, but what about
T
? Since
T
is a type and we want
T
to compute, it cannot live at layer
c
, but also
not
m
as it must be a well-formed pure MLTT type. Indeed,
T
ought to live at layer
d
, i.e.
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
. What about
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
? In this case,
t
lives at layer
d
. Since
T
must still be a well-formed pure MLTT type and compute, we must have

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
. The type of a term living at layer
m
simply also lives at layer
m
. The relation of layers of terms and types is summarized by the following function:

\displaystyle\Uparrow(\textsc{v})

\displaystyle:=\textsc{d}

\displaystyle\Uparrow(\textsc{c})

\displaystyle:=\textsc{d}

\displaystyle\Uparrow(\textsc{d})

\displaystyle:=\textsc{d}

\displaystyle\Uparrow(\textsc{m})

\displaystyle:=\textsc{m}

The judgment
i~\textsf{computable}
quantifies computable layers:

\frac{}{\textsc{d}~\textsf{computable}}\par\frac{}{\textsc{m}~\textsf{computable}}

Then we extend our system with five types, following
Sec.

2
:

•

U^{\delta}
is a global variable for types. Due to separation of types and terms, we need a way to refer to code of types on the
type level.

•

(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T
is a meta-function type for introducing a context variable
g

to the global context. We also have this in
Sec.

2
.

•

(U:(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T
is similarly a meta-function type for introducing a type at
layer
d
to the global context. This type is introduced to provide an index for the contextual type for terms to be
discussed in the second next item.

•

\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})
is a contextual type for types in MLTT. It represents a static code of types.

•

Finally,
\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})
is a contextual type for terms in MLTT. This
T
may refer to the index type at layer
d
introduced by meta-functions above.

Since there are two kinds of contextual types now, there four kinds of bindings in a
global context:

•

context variables
g:\textsf{Ctx}
representing a local context;

•

global variables
U:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})
representing a type in MLTT (note that
i\in\{\textsc{c},\textsc{d}\}
);

•

global variables
u:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})
representing a term in MLTT (note that

i\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}
and there is no way to introduce a term at layer
d
to global
context).

Now, let us move on to discuss the extended terms.

•

First, we also introduce global variables and local substitutions. Their syntax is identical to one in
Sec.

2
.

•

Then we have the introduction and elimination forms for meta-functions of
context variables,
\Lambda^{l}~{g}.t
and
t~\$~\Gamma
.

•

Similarly, we have the introduction and elimination forms for meta-functions of
types,
\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.t
and
t~\$_{p}~T
.

•

Then we have the introduction forms of two kinds of contextual types.

•

Following
Hu and Pientka (2024a, Sec. 4)
, we have two elimination forms for each kind of
contextual types,
letbox
and the recursive principles. Same as before,
letbox
is responsible for code composition and evaluation. Intentional analyses are done through the recursive principles. In
DeLaM
,
letbox
is a bit more complex as it requires a specified motive. We alter the syntax a little bit to make
letbox
more like an operation:

\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M}~u\leftarrow(t:\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}
and
\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{t}.M}~u\leftarrow(t:\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}
.

•

Finally, we extend the recursive principles for code. As indicated before, code of types and terms in MLTT are mutually defined, so the
recursive principles must also be mutual. The two recursive principles
\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))
and

\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))
require two motives, one for types and one for terms,
and contain all branches for code of types and terms. Their difference is what exactly eventually being eliminated, as indicated by their
subscript. The branches
\overrightarrow{b}
are a list of branches
\overrightarrow{b}_{\textsf{Typ}}
and
\overrightarrow{b}_{\textsf{Trm}}
,
where
\overrightarrow{b}_{\textsf{Typ}}
and
\overrightarrow{b}_{\textsf{Trm}}
contain
all
branches for types and
terms, respectively.

In the branches, there are four kinds of variables.

•

There is a globally introduced context variable
g
which represents the
local context where the code lives.

•

There could be some universe variables that are used to tell the universe levels
of some types.

•

There could be some global variables
u
and
U
represent the sub-structures of
a given case. The subscripts correspond tightly to the syntax given in
Sec.

4.2
.

•

For each sub-structure, there is one corresponding recursive variable
x
. Again, the subscripts correspond tightly to the sub-structure.

4.6.
More Typing and Equivalence Judgments

In this section, we specify the remainder of the rules. We begin with the well-formedness rule for the global contexts. Recall that layer
v
does not apply for most rules below, unless the otherwise is
specifically stated.

\frac{}{L\vdash\cdot}\par\frac{L\vdash\Psi}{L\vdash\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx}}\par\frac{L\vdash\Psi\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Gamma\quad\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad i\in\{\textsc{c},\textsc{d}\}}{L\vdash\Psi,U:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})}\par\frac{L\vdash\Psi\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad i\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}}{L\vdash\Psi,u:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})}

The judgments for local substitutions follow very closely
Sec.

2
. In these rules,
i
might take
v
. This permission has a particular effect on the step case, which forces all terms in a
local substitution must be variables.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma\quad\quad\text{$\Gamma$ ends with $\cdot$}\quad|\Gamma|=k}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\cdot^{k}:\cdot\\
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\cdot^{k}\approx\cdot^{k}:\cdot}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma\quad g:\textsf{Ctx}\in\Psi\quad\quad\text{$\Gamma$ ends with $g$}\quad|\Gamma|=k}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\cdot_{g}^{k}:\cdot\\
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\cdot_{g}^{k}\approx\cdot_{g}^{k}:\cdot}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma\quad g:\textsf{Ctx}\in\Psi\quad\text{$\Gamma$ ends with $g$}\quad|\Gamma|=k}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\textsf{wk}_{g}^{k}:g\\
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\textsf{wk}_{g}^{k}\approx\textsf{wk}_{g}^{k}:g}\par\frac{L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta,t/x:\Delta,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta,t/x\approx\delta^{\prime},t^{\prime}/x:\Delta,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

In the step case for equivalence above, we ask for two redundant premises of the
well-typedness of
t
and
t^{\prime}
to provide an early presupposition for equivalence of
local substitutions. We will need this early presupposition in
Lemma

5.15
. It breaks the dependency loop so that reaching the full presupposition lemma becomes
viable.

Now let us consider the extended types and their equivalence.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma\quad U:(\Delta\vdash_{i^{\prime}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\in\Psi\quad i^{\prime}\in\{\textsc{c},\textsc{d}\}\quad i^{\prime}\leq i\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}U^{\delta}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad\quad L\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}

Notice that contextual types always live on the universe level
0
. This is fine because the meta-language at layer
m
is strictly more expressive than
that at layer
c
, i.e.
MLTT
. Therefore, the universe level
0
is large enough to encode all intrinsically typed
syntax of
MLTT
without causing any size issue. This intuition is further justified in the logical relations, where the semantics of
contextual types need not to refer to other semantics at layer
m
, so contextual
types can safely reside on level
0
. Similarly, meta-functions with open types as inputs also do not need to be concerned
about the universe levels of the types.

The additional equivalence rules are just their congruence rules:

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma\quad U:(\Delta\vdash_{i^{\prime}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\in\Psi\quad i^{\prime}\in\{\textsc{c},\textsc{d}\}\quad i^{\prime}\leq i\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}U^{\delta}\approx U^{\delta^{\prime}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\approx(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\vdash l_{1}\approx l_{3}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l_{2}\approx l_{4}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}))\Rightarrow^{l_{2}}T\approx(U:(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{3}$}))\Rightarrow^{l_{4}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}\quad L\vdash l\approx L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\approx\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}

Next, we list the extended typing judgments:

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma\quad u:(\Delta\vdash_{i^{\prime}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\in\Psi\quad i^{\prime}\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}\quad i\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c},\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}\quad i^{\prime}\leq i\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.t:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad T:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\texttt{box}\ t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}\par\frac{L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}:M[\texttt{box}\ U/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M}~U\leftarrow(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}:M[t/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}\par\frac{L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x_{t}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,u:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}:M[\texttt{box}\ u/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{t}.M}~u\leftarrow(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}:M[t/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

For the typing rule of
t~\$~\Delta
, we require
\Delta
to be a context at layer

d
. This is because a context variable represents a local context for an MLTT term. A local context for an MLTT term necessarily lives at layer
d
, so we can only
substitute a context living at layer
d
with a context variable.

Now we shall mentally prepare ourselves to write down the typing rules for the two
recursive principles. They are conceptually easy but simply verbose to write down. We will only write down the rules for this time for completeness and in later
discussions, we simply omit the premises. Our goal is to provide the following conclusions:

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})):M_{\textsf{Typ}}[l^{\prime}/l,\Delta/g,t/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})):M_{\textsf{Trm}}[l^{\prime}/l,\Delta/g,T/U_{T},t/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

We group the premises into different parts. First we give the premises related to the motives:

L\vdash l_{1}:\textsf{Level}\par L\vdash l_{2}:\textsf{Level}\par L,\ell\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M_{\textsf{Typ}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\par L,\ell\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx},U_{T}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$});\Gamma,x_{t}:\square(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}U_{T}^{\textsf{id}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M_{\textsf{Trm}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

where
\overrightarrow{M}=(\ell,g,x_{T}.M_{\textsf{Typ}})~(\ell,g,U_{T},x_{t}.M_{\textsf{Trm}})
. In the premises above, we give the well-formedness of two motives for code of types
and terms, respectively. Let us call this group
G_{M}
. We move on to considering the branches. We first consider the branches for code of types. It is relatively easy as there are only four cases:

•

L\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\texttt{Nat}}:M_{\textsf{Typ}}[0/\ell,g/g,\texttt{box}\ \texttt{Nat}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

•

We explain this premise more carefully. Consider some code of type

\texttt{box}\ \Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T
, then we have the matching premise

L,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\Pi}:M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\ell\sqcup\ell^{\prime}/\ell,g/g,\texttt{box}\ \Pi^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(x:U_{S}^{\textsf{id}}).U_{T}^{\textsf{id}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

where

\displaystyle\Psi^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle~\Psi

,

\displaystyle~g:\textsf{Ctx}

(the global variable for the input type, which captures
S
)

,

\displaystyle~U_{S}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})

(the global variable for the output type, which captures
T
; note that it
lives in an extended local context)

,

\displaystyle~U_{T}:(g,x:U_{S}^{\textsf{id}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell^{\prime}$})

and

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle~\Gamma

(the recursive call for
S
of type
M
that is properly substituted)

,

\displaystyle~x_{S}:M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\ell/\ell,g/g,\texttt{box}\ U_{S}^{\textsf{id}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

(the recursive call for
T
; see how the local context is extended)

,

\displaystyle~x_{T}:M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\ell^{\prime}/\ell,(g,x:U_{S}^{\textsf{id}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})/g,\texttt{box}\ U_{T}^{\textsf{id}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

•

Further,

L,\ell\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\texttt{Ty}}:M_{\textsf{Typ}}[1+\ell/\ell,g/g,\texttt{box}\ \texttt{Ty}_{\ell}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

•

L,\ell\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx},u_{t}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\texttt{Ty}_{\ell}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+\ell$});\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\texttt{El}}:M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\ell/\ell,g/g,\texttt{box}\ (\texttt{El}^{\ell}~u_{t}^{\textsf{id}})/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

where

\Gamma^{\prime}:=\Gamma,x_{t}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[1+\ell/\ell,g/g,\texttt{Ty}_{\ell}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

Let us call this group
G_{\textsf{Typ}}
.

Lastly, let us consider the nine cases for terms.

•

L,\ell\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx},U_{T}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$}),u_{x}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{v}}U_{T}^{\textsf{id}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{x}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[\ell/\ell,g/g,U_{T}^{\textsf{id}}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ u_{x}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

In this case, the type of the variable is captured by
U_{T}
. It has to live at layer
d
because it is not a sub-structure of the variable,
i.e. it is obtained externally, from the indexing arguments of the recursive principles.

•

L\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}_{\texttt{Nat}}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[1/\ell,g/g,\texttt{Ty}_{0}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ \texttt{Nat}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

•

L,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}_{\Pi}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[1+{(\ell\sqcup\ell^{\prime})}/\ell,g/g,\texttt{Ty}_{\ell\sqcup\ell^{\prime}}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ \Pi^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(x:u_{s}^{\textsf{id}}).u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

where

\Psi^{\prime}:=\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx},u_{s}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\texttt{Ty}_{\ell}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+\ell$}),u_{t}:(g,x:\texttt{El}^{\ell}~u_{s}^{\textsf{id}}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\texttt{Ty}_{\ell^{\prime}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+\ell^{\prime}$})

and

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle~\Gamma

,

\displaystyle~x_{s}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[1+\ell/\ell,g/g,\texttt{Ty}_{\ell}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ u_{s}^{\textsf{id}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

,

\displaystyle~x_{t}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[1+\ell^{\prime}/\ell,(g,x:\texttt{El}^{\ell}~u_{s}^{\textsf{id}})/g,\texttt{Ty}_{\ell^{\prime}}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

Notice that this premise for the encoding of
\Pi
is almost identical to the
premise in
G_{\textsf{Typ}}
above, with necessary adjustment to return the proper motive

M_{\textsf{Trm}}
instead.

•

L,\ell\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}_{\texttt{Ty}}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[{2+\ell}/\ell,g/g,\texttt{Ty}_{1+\ell}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ \texttt{Ty}_{\ell}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

•

L\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\textsf{zero}}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[0/\ell,g/g,\texttt{Nat}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ \textsf{zero}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

•

L\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx},u_{t}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$});\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\textsf{succ}}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[0/\ell,g/g,\texttt{Nat}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ (\textsf{succ}~u_{t}^{\textsf{id}})/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

where
\Gamma^{\prime}:=\Gamma,x_{t}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[0/\ell,g/g,\texttt{Nat}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}
.

•

We carefully explain this premise for the code of the elimination of natural
numbers. Recall that the syntax is
\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~t
. We use corresponding global variables to capture the sub-structures.

L,\ell\;|\;\Psi^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[\ell/\ell,g/g,U_{M}^{\textsf{id}_{g},u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/x}/U_{T},\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{\ell}~(x.U_{M}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x}})~u_{s}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}~(x,y.u_{s^{\prime}}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x,y}})~u_{t}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

where

\displaystyle\Psi^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle~\Psi

,

\displaystyle~g:\textsf{Ctx}

(the global variable for the motive; it lives at layer
c
as it is a sub-structure)

,

\displaystyle~U_{M}:(g,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})

(the code for the base case; its type refers to the code of the motive with

x
for
zero
)

,

\displaystyle~u_{s}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}U_{M}^{\textsf{id}_{g},\textsf{zero}/x}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})

(the code for the step case; the local context is extended with the
predecessor and the recursive call)

,

\displaystyle~u_{s^{\prime}}:(g,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$},y:U_{M}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}U_{M}^{\textsf{id}_{g},\textsf{succ}~x/x}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})

(the code for the scrutinee)

,

\displaystyle~u_{t}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$})

and

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle~\Gamma

(since the motive is a sub-structure, a recursive call is available)

,

\displaystyle~x_{M}:M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\ell/\ell,(g,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$})/g,\texttt{box}\ U_{M}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

(the recursive call for the base case; recall that
U_{T}
is the type of

x_{t}
,)

,

\displaystyle~x_{s}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[\ell/\ell,g/g,U_{M}^{\textsf{id}_{g},\textsf{zero}/x}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ u_{s}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

(the recursive call for the step case; similar logic applies but more longer)

,

\displaystyle~x_{s^{\prime}}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[\ell/\ell,(g,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$},y:U_{M}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})/g,U_{M}^{\textsf{id}_{g},\textsf{succ}~x/x}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ u_{s^{\prime}}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x,y}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

(the recursive call for the scrutinee)

,

\displaystyle~x_{t}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[\ell/\ell,g/g,\texttt{Nat}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

•

L,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\lambda}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[\ell\sqcup\ell^{\prime}/\ell,g/g,\Pi^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(x:U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}).U_{T}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x}}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ \lambda^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(x:U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}).u_{t}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

where

\displaystyle\Psi^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle~\Psi

,

\displaystyle~g:\textsf{Ctx}

,

\displaystyle~U_{S}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})

,

\displaystyle~U_{T}:(g,x:U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$}\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell^{\prime}$})

,

\displaystyle~u_{t}:(g,x:U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}U_{T}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell^{\prime}$})

and

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle~\Gamma

,

\displaystyle~x_{S}:M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\ell/\ell,g/g,\texttt{box}\ U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

,

\displaystyle~x_{t}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[\ell^{\prime}/\ell,(g,x:U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})/g,U_{T}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x}}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ u_{t}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

Note that here
U_{T}
is at layer
d
. This is because the return type of not a sub-structure in a function abstraction

\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).t
, and therefore it must be captured externally from the indexing
arguments of the recursive principle. Since it is not a sub-structure, there also is not a recursive call for it. It is possible to include the return type as a sub-structure, e.g.
\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).(t:T)
but we decided to show this alternative to demonstrate various design spaces.

•

Finally,

L,\ell,\ell^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi^{\prime};\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\textsf{app}}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[\ell^{\prime}/\ell,g/g,U_{T}^{\textsf{id}_{g},u_{t}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}/x}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ ((u_{t}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}:\Pi^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(x:U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}).U_{T}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x}})~u_{s}^{\textsf{id}_{g}})/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

where

\displaystyle\Psi^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle~\Psi

,

\displaystyle~g:\textsf{Ctx}

,

\displaystyle~U_{S}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})

,

\displaystyle~U_{T}:(g,x:U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell^{\prime}$})

,

\displaystyle~u_{t}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\Pi^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(x:U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}).U_{T}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell\sqcup\ell^{\prime}$})

,

\displaystyle~u_{s}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})

and

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime}:=

\displaystyle~\Gamma

,

\displaystyle~x_{S}:M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\ell/\ell,g/g,\texttt{box}\ U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

,

\displaystyle~x_{T}:M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\ell/\ell,(g,x:U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})/g,\texttt{box}\ U_{T}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

,

\displaystyle~x_{t}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[\ell\sqcup\ell^{\prime}/\ell,g/g,\Pi^{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}(x:U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}).U_{T}^{\textsf{id}_{g,x}}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ u_{t}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

,

\displaystyle~x_{s}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[\ell/\ell,g/g,U_{S}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ u_{s}^{\textsf{id}_{g}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

This premise shows why we must use a more verbose syntax for application, i.e.
(t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~s
. In the global context, we must introduce the global variables for the input and
output types. However, a vanilla function application
t~s
has no such information at all. Since the current syntax has both input and output types as sub-structures, we can
also allow their recursive calls.

All premises above conclude the group for terms, which we name
G_{\textsf{Trm}}
. We collectively use
G_{A}
for all three groups above, i.e.
G_{A}:=G_{M}~G_{\textsf{Typ}}~G_{\textsf{Trm}}
. Then we have the typing rule for the recursive principles as follows:

\frac{G_{A}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})):M_{\textsf{Typ}}[l^{\prime}/\ell,\Delta/g,t/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}\par\frac{G_{A}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})):M_{\textsf{Trm}}[l^{\prime}/\ell,\Delta/g,T/U_{T},t/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}

4.7.
More Congruence Rules for Typing

The congruence rules for the additional typing rules are naturally derived from
the typing rules above.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma\quad u:(\Delta\vdash_{i^{\prime}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\in\Psi\quad i^{\prime}\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}\quad i\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c},\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}\quad i^{\prime}\leq i\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}u^{\delta}\approx u^{\delta^{\prime}}:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Lambda^{l}~{g}.t\approx\Lambda^{l^{\prime}}~{g}.t^{\prime}:(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t~\$~\Delta\approx t^{\prime}~\$~\Delta^{\prime}:T[\Delta/g]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}\quad L\vdash l_{1}\approx l_{3}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l_{2}\approx l_{4}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Lambda^{l_{1},l_{2}}_{p}~{U}.t\approx\Lambda^{l_{3},l_{4}}_{p}~{U}.t^{\prime}:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}))\Rightarrow^{l_{2}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t~\$_{p}~T\approx t^{\prime}~\$_{p}~T^{\prime}:T^{\prime\prime}[T/U]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

The following rules are related to meta-programming and intensional analysis.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\texttt{box}\ T\approx\texttt{box}\ T^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\texttt{box}\ t\approx\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\vdash l_{1}\approx l_{3}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l_{2}\approx l_{4}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M\approx M^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{1}\approx t_{2}:M[\texttt{box}\ U^{\textsf{id}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\texttt{letbox}^{l_{1}}_{x_{T}.M}~U\leftarrow(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}))~\textsf{in}~t_{1}\approx\texttt{letbox}^{l_{3}}_{x_{T}.M^{\prime}}~U\leftarrow(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{4}$}))~\textsf{in}~t_{2}:M[t/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\vdash l_{1}\approx l_{3}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l_{2}\approx l_{4}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M\approx M^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,u:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{1}\approx t_{2}:M[\texttt{box}\ u^{\textsf{id}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\texttt{letbox}^{l_{1}}_{x_{t}.M}~u\leftarrow(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}))~\textsf{in}~t_{1}\approx\texttt{letbox}^{l_{3}}_{x_{T}.M^{\prime}}~u\leftarrow(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{4}$}))~\textsf{in}~t_{2}:M[t/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}

We omit the congruence rules for the recursive principles for code as they are
conceptually simple but too long. We simply let equivalence to propagate inwards to all the sub-terms of the recursive
principles.

4.8.
Computation Rules

Finally, we list all the computation rules. In the rules below, we let
i~\textsf{computable}
. We first list the
\beta
rules for
natural numbers:

\frac{L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{i}M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}s:M[\textsf{zero}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$},y:M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}s^{\prime}:M[\textsf{succ}~x/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}s\approx\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~\textsf{zero}:M[\textsf{zero}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{i}M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}s:M[\textsf{zero}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$},y:M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}s^{\prime}:M[\textsf{succ}~x/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}s^{\prime}[t/x,\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~t/y]\approx\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~(\textsf{succ}~t):M[\textsf{succ}~t/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

Then we have the
\beta
and
\eta
rules for dependent functions:

\frac{L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}s:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t[s/x]\approx(\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~s:T[s/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}\par\frac{L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).(t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~x\approx t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}}

In the
\eta
rule, on the right hand side, all
t
,
S
and
T
should be properly
locally weakened.

Finally we have
\beta
and
\eta
rules for universe-polymorphic functions:

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad|\overrightarrow{\ell}|=|\overrightarrow{l}|>0\quad\forall l^{\prime}\in\overrightarrow{l}~.~L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]\approx(\Lambda^{l}~{\overrightarrow{\ell}}.t)~\$~\overrightarrow{l}:T[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\omega$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Lambda^{l}~{\overrightarrow{\ell}}.(t~\$~\overrightarrow{\ell})\approx t:\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\omega$}}

Similarly, in the
\eta
rule, the universe variables appearing in
t
must also be
properly weakened.
This concludes all the rules for the MLTT portion of
DeLaM
.

Then we move on to considering the computation rules for the extended types. Let us finish considering all meta-function types.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[\Delta/g]\approx(\Lambda^{l}~{g}.t)~\$~\Delta:T[\Delta/g]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Lambda^{l}~{g}.(t~\$~g)\approx t:(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[T/U]\approx(\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.t)~\$_{p}~T:T^{\prime}[T/U]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.(t~\$_{p}~U^{\textsf{id}})\approx t:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

Now we consider the contextual types. They only have
\beta
rules. Let us consider
letbox
first.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}:M[\texttt{box}\ U^{\textsf{id}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}[T/U]\approx\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M}~U\leftarrow((\texttt{box}\ T):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}:M[\texttt{box}\ T/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x_{t}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,u:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}:M[\texttt{box}\ u^{\textsf{id}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}[t/u]\approx\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{t}.M}~u\leftarrow((\texttt{box}\ t):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}:M[\texttt{box}\ t/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

We can also give the
\beta
rules for the recursive principles for code. There are too many to list them all, and moreover they follow the same pattern, so we
just list a selected few of them. We begin with something easy:

\frac{G_{A}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\texttt{Nat}}[\Delta/g]\approx\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ \texttt{Nat}):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$})):M[0/\ell,\Delta/g,\texttt{box}\ \texttt{Nat}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}

In this case, we provide
Nat
to the recursive principle for code of types. It hits the base case described by
t_{\texttt{Nat}}
, and thus the whole program is reduced to

t_{\texttt{Nat}}
with
g
for
\Delta
. Note that the universe level for
Nat
must be
0
as specified by the typing
judgment at layer
c
. The recursive principle for code of terms behaves very similarly when encountering the
code of
Nat
. Instead, it picks the right branch
t^{\prime}_{\texttt{Nat}}
and returns the right motive instead:

\frac{G_{A}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}_{\texttt{Nat}}[\Delta/g]\approx\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ \texttt{Nat}):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\texttt{Ty}_{0}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1$})):M_{\textsf{Trm}}[1/\ell,\Delta/g,\texttt{Ty}_{0}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ \texttt{Nat}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}

In order to have the code to be
box

Nat
as a term, this code must have type
\texttt{Ty}_{0}
, which lives at universe level
1
. Hence the indices are forced by the typing rules at layer
c
.

Then we specify the variable case:

\frac{G_{A}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\in\Delta}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{x}[l^{\prime}/\ell,\Delta/g,T/U_{T},x/u_{x}]\approx\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ x):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})):M_{\textsf{Trm}}[l^{\prime}/\ell,\Delta/g,T/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ x/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}

The subtlety here is that
u_{x}
can only receive a variable as it is typed at layer

v
, but it is fine as
x
is precisely just a variable.

Then let us consider a more complex case of
\Pi
types.

\frac{G_{A}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad t=\texttt{box}\ \Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\quad s_{S}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ S):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\quad s_{T}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ T):\square((\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\Pi}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime},\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T},s_{S}/x_{S},s_{T}/x_{T}]\approx\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~(l\sqcup l^{\prime})$})):M_{\textsf{Trm}}[l\sqcup l^{\prime}/\ell,\Delta/g,t/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}

Notice how
s_{S}
and
s_{T}
recurse down the sub-structures, i.e.
S
and
T
with the
proper universe levels and local contexts. We end our discussion by given the
\beta
rules for code of function abstractions and
applications, as they appear to be rather complex, but their essence is fundamentally simple.

\frac{G_{A}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad l_{\Pi}=l\sqcup l^{\prime}\quad T_{\Pi}=\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\quad t^{\prime}=\texttt{box}\ \lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).t\quad s_{S}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ S):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\quad s_{t}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ t):\square((\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))\quad\delta=s_{S}/x_{S},s_{t}/x_{t}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\lambda}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime},\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T},t/u_{t},\delta]\approx\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T_{\Pi}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{\Pi}$})):M_{\textsf{Trm}}[l_{\Pi}/\ell,\Delta/g,T_{\Pi}/U_{T},t^{\prime}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}

Similarly, the recursive principle for code of terms picks the right branch
(
t_{\lambda}
) with variables properly substituted. Since
S
is also a sub-structure, the recursive call
s_{S}
invokes the recursive
principle for code of types instead, hence making the recursive principles mutually
defined.

Last, we give the case for function applications.

\frac{G_{A}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}s:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad T_{\textsf{app}}=T[s/x]\quad t^{\prime}=\texttt{box}\ ((t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~s)\quad s_{S}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ S):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\quad s_{T}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ T):\square((\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))\quad s_{t}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ t):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}(\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~(l\sqcup l^{\prime})$}))\quad s_{s}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ s):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\quad\sigma=\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T},t/u_{t},s/u_{s}\quad\delta=s_{S}/x_{S},s_{T}/x_{T},s_{t}/x_{t},s_{s}/x_{s}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\textsf{app}}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime},\sigma,\delta]\approx\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T_{\textsf{app}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})):M_{\textsf{Trm}}[l^{\prime}/\ell,\Delta/g,T_{\textsf{app}}/U_{T},t^{\prime}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}

Similar to above, we can do recursion on all sub-structures, including
S
and
T
,
which are handled by the recursive principles for code of types. It is not only convenient to put
S
and
T
in the syntax of a function
application, but also necessary. If we look at
s_{t}
and
s_{s}
, the recursive calls on the function and the argument,
we see that we must supply their types, i.e.
\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T
and
S
, respectively. This information, unfortunately, cannot be recovered, if we employed the more common
syntax of
t~s
. In practice, the
\Pi
type can be filled in by a type inference algorithm when we do
not care, so it does not truly make the type theory more difficult, but rather enables
the recursion on code of function applications.

At this point, we conclude all rules for
DeLaM
. Next, we shall carefully define all syntactic operations and examine the syntactic
properties of
DeLaM
. Then we work out the semantics by following
Sec.

2
and

Abel
et al. (2017)
, from which we conclude the convertibility problem of

DeLaM
is decidable.

4.9.
A Note on Layer
v
Rules

To summarize, only the following rules can be indexed by layer
v
:

•

the typing rule for local variables and its congruence;

•

the local substitution rules and their equivalence rules;

•

the typing rule for global variables and its congruence;

•

all conversion rules for terms and their equivalence.

In particular, we are not even obliged to include symmetry and transitivity, because
they can be derived from existing rules.

5.
Syntactic Operations and Properties of
DeLaM

In the previous section, we have introduced all judgments of
DeLaM
, but we have
left out some details. For one, we have not defined the substitution operations yet, though they are very
intuitive. For the sake of completeness, we will give their definitions. Then we examine the syntactic properties of
DeLaM
before entering the semantic
zone.

5.1.
Substitution Operations

In
Sec.

4.3
, we have given the definition of substitutions for universe
levels and how to apply one to a universe level. Applying a substitution for universe levels to types and terms simply propagate the
substitution downwards.

The composition operation and the identity substitution are defined intuitively as:

\cdot
~\circ~\phi~:=~

\cdot

(\phi^{\prime},l/\ell)
~\circ~\phi~:=~

(\phi^{\prime}\circ\phi),l[\phi]/\ell

\textsf{id}_{\cdot}
~:=~

\cdot

\textsf{id}_{L,\ell}
~:=~

\textsf{id}_{L},\ell/\ell

The presentation of the identity substitution is simpler as we do not consider
weakenings for universe contexts. We sometimes omit the subscript when it can be inferred from the textual context. We will also need to apply a universe substitution to global context, which does not
need to be mutually defined:

\cdot
[\phi]~:=~

\cdot

(\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx})
[\phi]~:=~

\Psi[\phi],g:\textsf{Ctx}

(\Psi,U:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))

(\Psi,u:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))
[\phi]~:=~

\Psi[\phi],u:(\Gamma[\phi]\vdash_{i}T[\phi]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\phi]$})

Sec.

substitution in order to define its composition. We repeat their definitions as follows:

\displaystyle\widehat{\cdot_{g?}^{k}}

\displaystyle:=k

\displaystyle:=k

\displaystyle\widehat{\delta,t/x}

\displaystyle:=\widehat{\delta}

\displaystyle:=g?

\displaystyle:=g

\displaystyle:=\widecheck{\delta}

(\ell,g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\texttt{El}}[\delta,x_{t}/x_{t}])

(\ell,g,U_{T},u_{x}.t_{x})
[\delta]~:=~

(\ell,g,U_{T},u_{x}.t_{x}[\delta])

(g.t^{\prime}_{\texttt{Nat}})
[\delta]~:=~

(g.t^{\prime}_{\texttt{Nat}}[\delta])

(\ell,\ell^{\prime},g,u_{s},u_{t},x_{s},x_{t}.t^{\prime}_{\Pi})
[\delta]~:=~

(\ell,\ell^{\prime},g,u_{s},u_{t},x_{s},x_{t}.t^{\prime}_{\Pi}[\delta,x_{s}/x_{s},x_{t}/x_{t}])

(g.t_{\textsf{zero}})
[\delta]~:=~

(g.t_{\textsf{zero}}[\delta])

(g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\textsf{succ}})

(\ell,g,U_{M},u_{s},u_{s^{\prime}},u_{t},x_{M},x_{s},x_{s^{\prime}},x_{t}.t_{\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}})
[\delta]~:=~

(\ell,g,U_{M},u_{s},u_{s^{\prime}},u_{t},x_{M},x_{s},x_{s^{\prime}},x_{t}.t_{\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}}[\delta,x_{M}/x_{M},x_{s}/x_{s},x_{s^{\prime}}/x_{s^{\prime}},x_{t}/x_{t}])

(\ell,\ell^{\prime},g,U_{S},U_{T},u_{t},x_{S},x_{t}.t_{\lambda})
[\delta]~:=~

(\ell,\ell^{\prime},g,U_{S},U_{T},u_{t},x_{S},x_{t}.t_{\lambda}[\delta,x_{S}/x_{S},x_{t}/x_{t}])

(\ell,\ell^{\prime},g,U_{S},U_{T},u_{t},u_{s},x_{S},x_{T},x_{t},x_{s}.t_{\textsf{app}})
[\delta]~:=~

(\ell,\ell^{\prime},g,U_{S},U_{T},u_{t},u_{s},x_{S},x_{T},x_{t},x_{s}.t_{\textsf{app}}[\delta,x_{S}/x_{S},x_{T}/x_{T},x_{t}/x_{t},x_{s}/x_{s}])

where composition is defined in the same way as
Sec.

2
:

\displaystyle\textsf{wk}_{g}^{k}\circ\delta

\displaystyle:=\textsf{wk}_{g}^{\widehat{\delta}}

\displaystyle\cdot^{k}\circ\delta

\displaystyle:=\cdot_{\widecheck{\delta}}^{\widehat{\delta}}

\displaystyle\cdot_{g}^{k}\circ\delta

\displaystyle:=\cdot_{g}^{\widehat{\delta}}

\displaystyle(\delta^{\prime},t/x)\circ\delta

\displaystyle:=(\delta^{\prime}\circ\delta),t[\delta]/x

The identity local substitution is characterized as a generalization of local
weakening
wk
.

\displaystyle\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\cdot}

\displaystyle:=\cdot^{k}

\displaystyle\textsf{wk}^{k}_{g}

\displaystyle:=\textsf{wk}_{g}^{k}

\displaystyle\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\Gamma,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

\displaystyle:=\textsf{wk}^{1+k}_{\Gamma},x/x

Identity is just
\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}:=\textsf{wk}^{0}_{\Gamma}
.

Then we give the global substitutions.

(Global substitutions)

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\cdot\;|\;\sigma,\Gamma/g\;|\;\sigma,T/U\;|\;\sigma,t/u

Then we define the typing rules as:

\frac{L\vdash\Psi}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\cdot:\cdot}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Gamma}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma,\Gamma/g:\Phi,g:\textsf{Ctx}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi\quad L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Gamma\quad\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad i\in\{\textsc{c},\textsc{d}\}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma,T/U:\Phi,u:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi\quad L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad i\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}t:T[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma,t/u:\Phi,u:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})}

(\ell,\ell^{\prime},g,U_{S},U_{T},u_{t},u_{s},x_{S},x_{T},x_{t},x_{s}.t_{\textsf{app}})
[\sigma]~:=~

(\ell,\ell^{\prime},g,U_{S},U_{T},u_{t},u_{s},x_{S},x_{T},x_{t},x_{s}.t_{\textsf{app}}[\sigma,g/g,U_{S}^{\textsf{id}}/U_{S},U_{T}^{\textsf{id}}/U_{T},u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/u_{t},u_{s}^{\textsf{id}}/u_{s}])

Following
Sec.

2
, we give the identity global substitution as a special case of
global weakenings, and composition.

\displaystyle\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\cdot}

\displaystyle:=\cdot

\displaystyle\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx}}

\displaystyle:=\textsf{wk}_{\Psi}^{1+k},g/g

\displaystyle\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\Psi,U:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})}

\displaystyle:=\textsf{wk}^{1+k}_{\Psi},U^{\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}}/U

\displaystyle\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\Psi,u:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})}

\displaystyle:=\textsf{wk}^{1+k}_{\Psi},u^{\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}}/u

As a special case, we have

\displaystyle\textsf{id}_{\Psi}:=\textsf{wk}^{0}_{\Psi}

Moreover, we have composition

\displaystyle\cdot\circ\sigma^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\cdot

\displaystyle(\sigma,\Gamma/g)\circ\sigma^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime}),\Gamma[\sigma^{\prime}]/g

\displaystyle(\sigma,T/U)\circ\sigma^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime}),T[\sigma^{\prime}]/U

\displaystyle(\sigma,t/u)\circ\sigma^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=(\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime}),t[\sigma^{\prime}]/u

5.2.
Properties of Substitutions

In the next step, we examine the algebraic properties of all substitutions. In the lemmas below, we always assume well-formedness or well-typedness of the
subjects in the lemmas, unless the lemmas are about typing. For conciseness, we do not spell out the conditions as they are routine.

Lemma 5.1.

•

L\vdash\textsf{id}_{L}:L

•

If
L\vdash\phi:L^{\prime}
and
L^{\prime}\vdash\phi^{\prime}:L^{\prime\prime}
, then

L\vdash\phi^{\prime}\circ\phi:L^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

Analyze the definition of identity and composition.
∎

Lemma 5.2 (Algebra of Universe Substitutions).

•

l[\phi][\phi^{\prime}]=l[\phi\circ\phi^{\prime}]
(
T
,
\Gamma
,
\delta
,
t
,
\Psi
resp.)

•

l[\textsf{id}]=l
(
T
,
\Gamma
,
\delta
,
t
,
\Psi
resp.)

•

\textsf{id}\circ\phi=\phi
and
\phi\circ\textsf{id}=\phi

•

(\phi_{1}\circ\phi_{2})\circ\phi_{3}=\phi_{1}\circ(\phi_{2}\circ\phi_{3})

Proof.

The proofs are routine; the first two statements are proved by induction on
l

first, and then mutual induction on all applications of universe substitutions. The last two we analyze the definition of composition.
∎

Similar lemmas hold for local and global substitutions.

Lemma 5.3.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,\Delta\vdash_{i}\textsf{wk}_{\Gamma}^{|\Delta|}:\Gamma

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}:\Gamma

Note that local substitutions permit
i=\textsc{v}
, so we have
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{v}}\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}:\Gamma
. This intuitively makes sense, as all terms in
\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}
are just local variables.

Lemma 5.4 (Algebra of Local Substitutions).

•

T[\delta][\delta^{\prime}]=T[\delta\circ\delta^{\prime}]
(
t
resp.)

•

T[\textsf{id}]=T
(
t
resp.)

•

\textsf{id}\circ\delta=\delta
and
\delta\circ\textsf{id}=\delta

•

(\delta_{1}\circ\delta_{2})\circ\delta_{3}=\delta_{1}\circ(\delta_{2}\circ\delta_{3})

Proof.

The first statement is mutually proved with associativity and by mutual
induction. The second statement is mutually proved with right identity and also by
mutual induction. When proving right identity, we realize that all extended local substitutions under
binders are identities.
∎

Then we reason about global substitutions.

Lemma 5.5.

•

L\;|\;\Psi,\Phi\vdash\textsf{wk}_{\Psi}^{|\Phi|}:\Psi

•

L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\textsf{id}_{\Psi}:\Psi

Lemma 5.6 (Algebra of Global Substitutions).

•

T[\sigma][\sigma^{\prime}]=T[\sigma\circ\sigma^{\prime}]
(
\Gamma
,
\delta
,
t
resp.)

•

T[\textsf{id}]=T
(
\Gamma
,
\delta
,
t
resp.)

•

\textsf{id}\circ\sigma=\sigma
and
\sigma\circ\textsf{id}=\sigma

•

(\sigma_{1}\circ\sigma_{2})\circ\sigma_{3}=\sigma_{1}\circ(\sigma_{2}\circ\sigma_{3})

Proof.

The first two statements require mutual inductions on the applications of global
substitutions. Right identity is a natural consequence of the second statement. Left identity is proved by simply looking at the definition of the identity global
substitution. Associativity is routine.
∎

Finally, we conclude how all these kinds of substitutions interact.

Lemma 5.7 (Acting on Weakenings).

•

\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\Gamma}[\phi]=\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\Gamma[\phi]}

•

\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\Psi}[\phi]=\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\Psi[\phi]}

•

\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\Gamma}[\sigma]=\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\Gamma[\sigma]}

Proof.

The first two statements are pretty straightforward as the lengths of the contexts
are not altered. The last one requires a bit more thought. We proceed by induction on
\Gamma
.

Case

\displaystyle\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\cdot}[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\cdot^{k}[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\cdot^{k}

Case

\displaystyle\textsf{wk}^{k}_{g}[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\sigma(g)}

which already matches the definition of
\textsf{wk}^{k}_{g[\sigma]}
.

Case

\displaystyle\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\Gamma,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}[\sigma]

\displaystyle=(\textsf{wk}^{1+\textsc{m}}_{\Gamma},x/x)[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\textsf{wk}^{1+\textsc{m}}_{\Gamma}[\sigma],x/x

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\textsf{wk}^{1+\textsc{m}}_{\Gamma[\sigma]},x/x

\displaystyle=\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\Gamma[\sigma],x:T[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

∎

Corollary 5.8 (Acting on Identities).

•

\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}[\phi]=\textsf{id}_{\Gamma[\phi]}

•

\textsf{id}_{\Psi}[\phi]=\textsf{id}_{\Psi[\phi]}

•

\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}[\sigma]=\textsf{id}_{\Gamma[\sigma]}

Lemma 5.9 (Interactions between Different Substitutions).

•

T[\delta][\phi]=T[\phi][\delta[\phi]]
(
t
, resp.)

•

(\delta\circ\delta^{\prime})[\phi]=(\delta[\phi])\circ(\delta^{\prime}[\phi])

•

T[\sigma][\phi]=T[\phi][\sigma[\phi]]
(
\Gamma
,
\delta
,
t
, resp.)

•

T[\delta][\sigma]=T[\sigma][\delta[\sigma]]
(
t
, resp.)

•

(\delta\circ\delta^{\prime})[\sigma]=(\delta[\sigma])\circ(\delta^{\prime}[\sigma])

Proof.

The first two statements are mutually proved. The last two statements are also
mutually proved.

Most of them can be done by simply following the IHs. We give a few examples.

Case

\displaystyle(\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).t)[\delta][\phi]

\displaystyle=\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S[\delta][\phi]).(t[\delta,x/x][\phi])

(by IH)

\displaystyle=\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S[\phi][\delta[\phi]]).(t[\phi][(\delta,x/x)[\phi]])

\displaystyle=\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S[\phi][\delta[\phi]]).(t[\phi][(\delta[\phi],x/x)])

\displaystyle=(\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).t)[\phi][\delta[\phi]]

Case

\displaystyle(\ell,g,x_{T}.M_{\textsf{Typ}})[\delta][\phi]

\displaystyle=(\ell,g,x_{T}.M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\delta,x_{T}/x_{T}][\phi,\ell/\ell])

(by IH)

\displaystyle=(\ell,g,x_{T}.M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\phi,\ell/\ell][(\delta,x_{T}/x_{T})[\phi,\ell/\ell]])

(
\delta[\phi,\ell/\ell]=\delta[\phi]
due to weakening of universe variables)

\displaystyle=(\ell,g,x_{T}.M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\phi,\ell/\ell][(\delta[\phi],x_{T}/x_{T})])

\displaystyle=(\ell,g,x_{T}.M_{\textsf{Typ}})[\phi][\delta[\phi]]

Case

\displaystyle(\ell,g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\texttt{El}})[\sigma][\phi]

\displaystyle=(\ell,g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\texttt{El}}[\sigma,g/g,u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/u_{t}][\phi,\ell/\ell])

(by IH)

\displaystyle=(\ell,g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\texttt{El}}[\phi,\ell/\ell][(\sigma,g/g,u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/u_{t})[\phi,\ell/\ell]])

(
\sigma
is universe weakened;
Lemma

5.7
)

\displaystyle=(\ell,g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\texttt{El}}[\phi,\ell/\ell][(\sigma[\phi],g/g,u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/u_{t})])

\displaystyle=(\ell,g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\texttt{El}})[\phi][\sigma[\phi]]

Case

\displaystyle\textsf{wk}^{k}_{g}[\sigma][\phi]

\displaystyle=\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\sigma(g)}[\phi]

(by
Lemma

5.7
)

\displaystyle=\textsf{wk}^{k}_{\sigma(g)[\phi]}

\displaystyle=\textsf{wk}^{k}_{g[\sigma[\phi]]}

\displaystyle=\textsf{wk}^{k}_{g}[\phi][\sigma[\phi]]

Case

Then we consider
\cdot^{k}_{g}
, and we case analyze
\Gamma:=\sigma(g)
:

Subcase

If
\Gamma
ends with
\cdot
.

\displaystyle\cdot^{k}_{g}[\sigma][\phi]=\cdot^{|\Gamma|+\textsc{m}}[\phi]=\cdot^{|\Gamma|+\textsc{m}}

Subcase

If
\Gamma
ends with
g^{\prime}
.

\displaystyle\cdot^{k}_{g}[\sigma][\phi]=\cdot^{|\Gamma|+\textsc{m}}_{g^{\prime}}[\phi]=\cdot^{|\Gamma|+\textsc{m}}_{g^{\prime}}

Case

\displaystyle(\ell,g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\texttt{El}})[\delta][\sigma]

\displaystyle=(\ell,g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\texttt{El}}[\delta,x_{t}/x_{t}][\sigma,u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/u_{t}])

(by IH)

\displaystyle=(\ell,g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\texttt{El}}[\sigma,u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/u_{t}][(\delta,x_{t}/x_{t})[\sigma,u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/u_{t}]])

(
\delta
is globally weakened)

\displaystyle=(\ell,g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\texttt{El}}[\sigma,u_{t}^{\textsf{id}}/u_{t}][\delta[\sigma],x_{t}/x_{t}])

\displaystyle=(\ell,g,u_{t},x_{t}.t_{\texttt{El}})[\sigma][\delta[\sigma]]

Case

Now we consider
\textsf{wk}^{k}_{g}\circ\delta
. This composition basically cancels out all terms from
\delta
and leave a
weakening behind. In this case, we know that
\delta
must end with
\textsf{wk}^{k^{\prime}}_{g}
for some
g
. Moreover, in order to compose, we have that
|\delta|=\textsc{m}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle(\textsf{wk}^{k}_{g}\circ\delta)[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\textsf{wk}^{\widehat{\delta}}_{g}[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\textsf{wk}^{k^{\prime}}_{\sigma(g)}

Moreover,

\displaystyle(\textsf{wk}^{k}_{g}[\sigma])\circ(\delta[\sigma])

\displaystyle=\textsf{wk}^{|\delta|}_{\sigma(g)}\circ(\delta[\sigma])

(
\textsf{wk}^{|\delta|}_{\sigma(g)}
projects away all leading terms kept by

\delta
so only
\textsf{wk}^{k^{\prime}}_{g}[\sigma]
is left)

\displaystyle=\textsf{wk}^{k^{\prime}}_{\sigma(g)}

Therefore two expressions are equal. Similar reasoning holds for the case of
\cdot^{k}_{g}\circ\delta
.

Case

Then we consider
\cdot^{k}
and case analyze
\widecheck{\delta}
.

Subcase

If
\widecheck{\delta}
is not a context variable, then
\delta
must end
with
\cdot^{k^{\prime}}
and also
|\delta|=\textsc{m}
due to well-typedness.

\displaystyle(\cdot^{k}\circ\delta)[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\cdot^{\widehat{\delta}}[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\cdot^{k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\cdot^{k^{\prime}}[\sigma]

\displaystyle=(\cdot^{k}[\sigma])\circ(\delta[\sigma])

Subcase

If
\widecheck{\delta}
is context variable
g
, then
\delta
must end
with
\cdot^{k^{\prime}}_{g}
and also
|\delta|=\textsc{m}
due to well-typedness.

\displaystyle(\cdot^{k}\circ\delta)[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\cdot^{\widehat{\delta}}_{g}[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\cdot^{k^{\prime}}_{g}[\sigma]

Moreover,

\displaystyle\cdot^{k}[\sigma]\circ(\delta[\sigma])

\displaystyle=\cdot^{k}\circ(\delta[\sigma])

\displaystyle=\cdot^{\widehat{\delta[\sigma]}}_{\widecheck{\delta[\sigma]}}

\displaystyle=\cdot^{|\sigma(g)|+k^{\prime}}_{\widecheck{\delta[\sigma]}}

Then we consider whether
\sigma(g)
ends with another context variable or
not.

Subsubcase

If
\sigma(g)
ends with
\cdot
, then

\displaystyle(\cdot^{k}\circ\delta)[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\cdot^{k^{\prime}}_{g}[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\cdot^{|\sigma(g)|+k^{\prime}}

and also
\delta[\sigma]
must also end with no global variable.

Subsubcase

If
\sigma(g)
ends with some
g^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle(\cdot^{k}\circ\delta)[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\cdot^{k^{\prime}}_{g}[\sigma]

\displaystyle=\cdot^{|\sigma(g)|+k^{\prime}}_{g^{\prime}}

Then
\widecheck{\delta[\sigma]}
must also return
g^{\prime}
.

∎

Lemma 5.10 (Universe Substitutions).

•

If
L^{\prime}\vdash\Psi
and
L\vdash\phi:L^{\prime}
, then
L\vdash\Psi[\phi]
.

•

If
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
and
L\vdash\phi:L^{\prime}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi[\phi]\vdash_{i}\Gamma[\phi]
.

•

If
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
and
L\vdash\phi:L^{\prime}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi[\phi]\vdash_{i}\Gamma[\phi]\approx\Delta[\phi]
.

•

If
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\vdash\phi:L^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi[\phi];\Gamma[\phi]\vdash_{i}T[\phi]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\phi]$}
.

•

If
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\vdash\phi:L^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi[\phi];\Gamma[\phi]\vdash_{i}T[\phi]\approx T^{\prime}[\phi]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\phi]$}
.

•

If
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\vdash\phi:L^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi[\phi];\Gamma[\phi]\vdash_{i}t[\phi]:T[\phi]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\phi]$}
.

•

If
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\vdash\phi:L^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi[\phi];\Gamma[\phi]\vdash_{i}t[\phi]\approx t^{\prime}[\phi]:T[\phi]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\phi]$}
.

•

If
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Gamma^{\prime}
and
L\vdash\phi:L^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi[\phi];\Gamma[\phi]\vdash_{i}\delta[\phi]:\Gamma^{\prime}[\phi]
.

•

If
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}
and
L\vdash\phi:L^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi[\phi];\Gamma[\phi]\vdash_{i}\delta[\phi]\approx\delta^{\prime}[\phi]:\Gamma^{\prime}[\phi]
.

Proof.
