Do a mutual induction. Most rules do not alter the universe context at all so they
are discharged naturally. When encountering the only changing cases, i.e. universe-polymorphic functions and
branches of the recursive principles for code, we extend the unvierse substitutions
with sufficient new universe variables before applying the IHs. When computation rules are encountered, we apply the composition lemma above before
applying the IHs.

We consider one complex computational rule in detail to illustrate the proof of the
lemma:

\frac{G_{A}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L^{\prime}\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L^{\prime}\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad t=\texttt{box}\ \Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\quad s_{S}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ S):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\quad s_{T}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ T):\square((\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))}{L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\Pi}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime},\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T},s_{S}/x_{S},s_{T}/x_{T}]\approx\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~(l\sqcup l^{\prime})$})):M_{\textsf{Typ}}[l\sqcup l^{\prime}/\ell,\Delta/g,t/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}

On the right hand side,
\phi
simply propagates. By IH, we can show that all premises with the universe-substituted sub-terms are
well-formed. For example, we have

L\;|\;\Psi[\phi];\Delta[\phi]\vdash_{\textsc{c}}S[\phi]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\phi]$}

and

L\;|\;\Psi[\phi];\Delta[\phi],x:S[\phi]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\phi]$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T[\phi]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}[\phi]$}

Now we consider the left hand side.

\displaystyle t_{\Pi}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime},\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T},s_{S}/x_{S},s_{T}/x_{T}][\phi]

(by
Lemma

5.9
)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle t_{\Pi}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime}][\phi][\Delta[\phi]/g,S[\phi]/U_{S},T[\phi]/U_{T},s_{S}[\phi]/x_{S},s_{T}[\phi]/x_{T}]

(by naturality of substitutions)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle t_{\Pi}[\phi,\ell/\ell,\ell^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime}][l[\phi]/\ell,l^{\prime}[\phi]/\ell^{\prime}][\Delta[\phi]/g,S[\phi]/U_{S},T[\phi]/U_{T},s_{S}[\phi]/x_{S},s_{T}[\phi]/x_{T}]

Note that
t_{\Pi}[\phi,\ell/\ell,\ell^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime}]
is precisely how
\phi
should be
propagated in
t_{\Pi}
.

Now we consider the type. A similar reasoning applies:

(by
Lemma

5.9
)

\displaystyle M_{\textsf{Typ}}[l\sqcup l^{\prime}/\ell,\Delta/g,t/x_{T}][\phi]

\displaystyle=M_{\textsf{Typ}}[l\sqcup l^{\prime}/\ell][\phi][\Delta[\phi]/g,t[\phi]/x_{T}]

(by naturality of substitutions)

\displaystyle=M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\phi,\ell/\ell][l[\phi]\sqcup l^{\prime}[\phi]/\ell][\Delta[\phi]/g,t[\phi]/x_{T}]

This equality verifies the resulting type is correct. The same rule ensures the equivalence lives in the universe level
l_{1}[\phi]
.
∎

Then we consider the properties of local substitutions.

Lemma 5.11 (Partial Presupposition).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
, then
L\vdash\Psi
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Delta
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi
, then
L\vdash\Psi
and
L\vdash\Phi
.

Proof.

Induction on their respective premises. Note that in the second statement, the
definition of
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
is adjusted so that a simple induction
would suffice. The third statement requires the extra premises added to the step case of the equivalence judgment.
∎

In fact the lemma above has given full presupposition for local contexts and their
equivalence. Therefore, in the forthcoming full presupposition lemma, we do not have to state these
cases.

Lemma 5.12 (Symmetry and Transitivity of Local Substitutions).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta^{\prime}\approx\delta:\Delta
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime\prime}:\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime\prime}:\Delta
.

Proof.

By induction.
∎

Lemma 5.13 (Reflexivity).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta:\Delta
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Gamma
.

Proof.

The first two statements are proved by symmetry and then transitivity. The third (fourth) statement is a natural consequence of the second (first, resp.) statement.
∎

Lemma 5.14 (Local Substitutions).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Gamma^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Gamma^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T[\delta]\approx T^{\prime}[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Gamma^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t[\delta]:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Gamma^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t[\delta]\approx t^{\prime}[\delta]:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Gamma^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta^{\prime}\circ\delta:\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}\delta^{\prime}\approx\delta^{\prime\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Gamma^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta^{\prime}\circ\delta\approx\delta^{\prime\prime}\circ\delta:\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

Do a mutual induction. Many cases go through naturally if their premises do not alter the local context. In the base cases, we use partial presupposition above to obtain
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
and
L\vdash\Psi
. The global variable cases depend on the composition of local substitutions. The local variable case depends on the reflexivity lemma.

We consider a few cases:

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t~\$_{p}~T\approx t^{\prime}~\$_{p}~T^{\prime}:T^{\prime\prime}[T/U]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[\delta]\approx t^{\prime}[\delta]:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}(T^{\prime\prime}[\delta])\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

(by the same congruence rule)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[\delta]~\$_{p}~T\approx t^{\prime}[\delta]~\$_{p}~T^{\prime}:T^{\prime\prime}[\delta][T/U]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

Notice that

T^{\prime\prime}[\delta][T/U]=T^{\prime\prime}[T/U][\delta[T/U]]=T^{\prime\prime}[T/U][\delta]

where the second equation holds because
\delta
is not typed in a context with

U
.

Case

\frac{G_{A}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad l_{\Pi}=l\sqcup l^{\prime}\quad T_{\Pi}=\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\quad t^{\prime}=\texttt{box}\ \lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).t\quad s_{S}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ S):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))[\delta]\quad s_{t}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ t):\square((\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))[\delta]\quad\delta^{\prime}=s_{S}/x_{S},s_{t}/x_{t}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\lambda}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime},\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T},t/u_{t},\delta^{\prime}]\approx\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T_{\Pi}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{\Pi}$})):M_{\textsf{Trm}}[l_{\Pi}/\ell,\Delta/g,T_{\Pi}/U_{T},t^{\prime}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}

In this case, we first apply IHs so that
\delta
is propagated into all premises
in
G_{A}
and we must reason about the left hand side and the result type.

\displaystyle t_{\lambda}[\delta,x_{S}/x_{S},x_{t}/x_{t}][l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime},\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T},t/u_{t},\delta^{\prime}]

(by
Lemma

5.9
)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle t_{\lambda}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime},\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T},t/u_{t}][\delta[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime},\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T},t/u_{t}],x_{S}/x_{S},x_{t}/x_{t}][\delta^{\prime}]

(
\delta
has no those variables)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle t_{\lambda}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime},\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T},t/u_{t}][\delta,x_{S}/x_{S},x_{t}/x_{t}][\delta^{\prime}]

(naturality of local substitutions)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle t_{\lambda}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime},\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T},t/u_{t}][s_{S}/x_{S},s_{t}/x_{t}][\delta]

On the return type, we have

\displaystyle M_{\textsf{Trm}}[\delta,x_{t}/x_{t}][l_{\Pi}/\ell,\Delta/g,T_{\Pi}/U_{T},t^{\prime}/x_{t}]

(by
Lemma

5.9
similarly)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle M_{\textsf{Trm}}[l_{\Pi}/\ell,\Delta/g,T_{\Pi}/U_{T}][\delta,x_{t}/x_{t}][t^{\prime}/x_{t}]

(naturality of local substitutions)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle M_{\textsf{Trm}}[l_{\Pi}/\ell,\Delta/g,T_{\Pi}/U_{T}][t^{\prime}/x_{t}][\delta]

Both equations conclude this case.

∎

We also need a similar lemma about equivalent local substitutions.

Lemma 5.15 (Equivalent Local Substitutions).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{2}:\Gamma^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T[\delta_{1}]\approx T[\delta_{2}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{2}:\Gamma^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T[\delta_{1}]\approx T^{\prime}[\delta_{2}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{2}:\Gamma^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t[\delta_{1}]\approx t[\delta_{2}]:T[\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{2}:\Gamma^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t[\delta_{1}]\approx t^{\prime}[\delta_{2}]:T[\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}\delta:\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{2}:\Gamma^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\circ\delta_{1}\approx\delta\circ\delta_{2}:\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{2}:\Gamma^{\prime}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\circ\delta_{1}\approx\delta^{\prime}\circ\delta_{2}:\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

We proceed by mutual induction. When we encounter well-formedness of types and well-typedness of terms, we conclude
by IHs and respective congruence rules. What is more difficult are the asymmetric equivalence rules. We must apply IHs a bit more carefully to obtain the conclusions. We elaborate on a few cases.

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}\approx t^{\prime\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

(by presupposition)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta_{1}:\Gamma^{\prime}

(by local substitution lemma)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t[\delta_{1}]\approx t^{\prime}[\delta_{1}]:T[\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}[\delta_{1}]\approx t^{\prime\prime}[\delta_{2}]:T[\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

(by transitivity)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t[\delta_{1}]\approx t^{\prime\prime}[\delta_{2}]:T[\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}t:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

(by presupposition)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta_{1}:\Gamma^{\prime}

(by local substitution lemma)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}T[\delta_{1}]\approx T^{\prime}[\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t[\delta_{1}]\approx t[\delta_{2}]:T^{\prime}[\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

(by conversion)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t[\delta_{1}]\approx t[\delta_{2}]:T[\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[T/U]\approx(\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.t)~\$_{p}~T:T^{\prime}[T/U]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

(by presupposition)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\delta_{1}:\Gamma^{\prime}

(by local substitution lemma)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[T/U][\delta_{1}]\approx(\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.t[\delta_{1}])~\$_{p}~T:T^{\prime}[T/U][\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[\delta_{1}]\approx t[\delta_{2}]:T^{\prime}[\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

(by congruence; note that
T^{\prime}[T/U][\delta_{1}]=T^{\prime}[\delta_{1}][T/U]
as

U
is not in
\delta_{1}
)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}(\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.t[\delta_{1}])~\$_{p}~T\approx(\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.t[\delta_{2}])~\$_{p}~T:T^{\prime}[T/U][\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

(by transitivity)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[T/U][\delta_{1}]\approx(\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.t[\delta_{2}])~\$_{p}~T:T^{\prime}[T/U][\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma^{\prime}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime},x_{T}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}:M[\texttt{box}\ U^{\textsf{id}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}[T/U]\approx\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M}~U\leftarrow((\texttt{box}\ T):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}:M[\texttt{box}\ T/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

(by presupposition)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\delta_{1}:\Gamma^{\prime}

(by presupposition)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma

(by local substitution lemma)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}[T/U][\delta_{1}]\approx\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M[\delta_{1},x_{T}/x_{T}]}~U\leftarrow((\texttt{box}\ T):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}[\delta_{1}]:M[\texttt{box}\ T/x_{T}][\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M[\delta_{1},x_{T}/x_{T}]\approx M[\delta_{2},x_{T}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}[\delta_{1}]\approx t^{\prime}[\delta_{2}]:M[\delta_{1},x_{T}/x_{T}][\texttt{box}\ U^{\textsf{id}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

\displaystyle\quad{\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M[\delta_{1},x_{T}/x_{T}]}~U\leftarrow((\texttt{box}\ T):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}[\delta_{1}]}

(by congruence)

\displaystyle\approx{\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M[\delta_{2},x_{T}/x_{T}]}~U\leftarrow((\texttt{box}\ T):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}[\delta_{2}]}:{M[\delta_{1},x_{T}/x_{T}][\texttt{box}\ T/x_{T}]}

(by transitivity)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}[T/U][\delta_{1}]\approx\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M[\delta_{2},x_{T}/x_{T}]}~U\leftarrow((\texttt{box}\ T):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}[\delta_{2}]:M[\delta_{1},x_{T}/x_{T}][\texttt{box}\ T/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

Case

Finally we consider a
\eta
rule.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Lambda^{l}~{g}.(t~\$~g)\approx t:(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

(by presupposition)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\delta_{1}:\Gamma^{\prime}

(by local substitution lemma)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[\delta_{1}]:(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}(T[\delta_{1}])\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

(by the same
\eta
rule)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Lambda^{l}~{g}.((t[\delta_{1}])~\$~g)\approx t[\delta_{1}]:(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}(T[\delta_{1}])\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[\delta_{1}]\approx t[\delta_{2}]:(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}(T[\delta_{1}])\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

(by transitivity)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Lambda^{l}~{g}.((t[\delta_{1}])~\$~g)\approx t[\delta_{2}]:(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}(T[\delta_{1}])\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

∎

Remark
.

Note that the statement of this lemma is left-biased. For example, when considering terms, the types are substituted by
\delta_{1}
. This bias causes the whole formulation of equivalence judgment between terms to be
left biased as well. Otherwise, this lemma cannot be easily justified as above and requires the global
substitution lemma, which definitely causes issues as the latter depends on this
very lemma in the global variable cases.

A visible effect of this left bias is especially evident in the computation rules. For example, if we define the following
\beta
rule instead, then the lemma above
suddenly becomes unprovable at this stage:

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}(\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.t)~\$_{p}~T\approx t[T/U]:T^{\prime}[T/U]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

Let us work on the proof to see what happens. We now must prove

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}(\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.(t[\delta_{1}]))~\$_{p}~T\approx t[T/U][\delta_{2}]:T^{\prime}[T/U][\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

The only way to introduce
\delta_{2}
on the right hand side now is to apply the
local substitution lemma, which yields

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}(\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.(t[\delta_{2}]))~\$_{p}~T\approx t[T/U][\delta_{2}]:T^{\prime}[T/U][{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\delta_{2}}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

Notice that the return type yields a local substitution
\delta_{2}
, instead of

\delta_{1}
as required by the goal. At this stage, however, we are not able to prove the equivalence between

T^{\prime}[T/U][\delta_{1}]
and
T^{\prime}[T/U][\delta_{2}]
as we are missing the global
substitution lemma to justify that
T^{\prime}[T/U]
remains well-formed.

Similarly, we are not able to prove a lemma if some
\eta
rules are flipped
either. Consider the following “innocent”
\eta
rule:

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx\Lambda^{l}~{g}.(t~\$~g):(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

Since there is only one premise, we have no choice but to eventually use IH to
obtain

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[\delta_{1}]\approx t[\delta_{2}]:(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}(T[\delta_{1}])\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

This leaves us
to prove

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[\delta_{2}]\approx\Lambda^{l}~{g}.((t[\delta_{2}])~\$~g):(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}(T[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\delta_{1}}])\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

Notice how the equivalence itself talks about
\delta_{2}
exclusively while the type
refers to
\delta_{1}
. This asymmetry forces us to flip the equivalence to obtain a better proof.

Then we move on to the global substitution lemma. We must first establish a number of other lemmas. The lifting lemma is one of the guiding lemmas of the layering principle, where we
require that well-formedness can be carried over to higher layers.

Lemma 5.16 (Lifting).

If
i\leq i^{\prime}
, and

•

L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i^{\prime}}\Gamma
;

•

L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i^{\prime}}\Gamma\approx\Delta
;

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i^{\prime}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i^{\prime}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i^{\prime}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i^{\prime}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i^{\prime}}\delta:\Delta
;

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i^{\prime}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
.

Proof.

First, we realize that the
\Uparrow
function is monotonic, i.e.
\Uparrow(i)\leq\Uparrow(i^{\prime})
. We proceed by a mutual induction. Most cases are obvious by IHs. Notice that there are cases where we have premises like
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma
, so we must apply IH to obtain
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i^{\prime})}\Gamma
with the
monotonicity property above. It works similarly for the conversion rule, where we have
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
. In the cases of global variables, the transitivity of
\leq
eventually complete the
proof of this lemma. We elaborate on one case:

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma\quad u:(\Delta\vdash_{i^{\prime\prime}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\in\Psi\quad i^{\prime\prime}\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}\quad i\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c},\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}\quad i^{\prime\prime}\leq i\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}u^{\delta}:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i^{\prime})}\Gamma

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i^{\prime}}\delta:\Delta

\displaystyle i^{\prime\prime}\leq i\leq i^{\prime}

(by the same rule)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i^{\prime}}u^{\delta}:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

∎

The inverse of lifting sometimes is possible

Lemma 5.17 (Unlifting).

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\delta:\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\delta:\Delta
;

Proof.

Induction. Notice that
\Uparrow(\textsc{d})=\Uparrow(\textsc{c})=\textsc{d}
.
∎

The unlifting lemma says that the typing at layer
d
can be unlifted back to layer

c
.

As another guiding lemma, we have the static code lemma, which states that code at
layer
v
and
c
has no computational behavior.

Lemma 5.18 (Static Code).

If
i\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
T=T^{\prime}
;

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
t=t^{\prime}
;

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
, then
\delta=\delta^{\prime}
.

All equalities above are quotient over the equivalence of universe levels.

Proof.

Mutual induction. We are not concerned about the equivalence of types due to the conversion rule.
∎

We emphasize again that the equalities hold modulo the equivalence of universe
levels. For example,
\texttt{Ty}_{\ell\sqcup\ell^{\prime}}
and
\texttt{Ty}_{\ell^{\prime}\sqcup\ell}
as code are
considered
equal
, though their universe levels are not exactly syntactically
identical. This is fine as we know how to decide the equality between two universe levels as
shown in
Sec.

4.3
.

Lemma 5.19 (Global Substitutions).

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
,
i\in\{\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma[\sigma]
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
,
i\in\{\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma[\sigma]\approx\Delta[\sigma]
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}T[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}T[\sigma]\approx T^{\prime}[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}t[\sigma]:T[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}t[\sigma]\approx t^{\prime}[\sigma]:T[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}\delta[\sigma]:\Delta[\sigma]
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}\delta[\sigma]\approx\delta^{\prime}[\sigma]:\Delta[\sigma]
.

Proof.

We proceed by a mutual induction. Notice that in the first two statements,
i\in\{\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}
, namely the range of the

\Uparrow
function. This ensures a lookup
\sigma(g)
of a context variable
g
to be well-formed at
layer
i
, due to
Lemma

5.16
. Most cases can be discharged by IHs directly. The complex cases are the computation rules and the global variable cases.

We consider a few cases:

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma\quad u:(\Delta\vdash_{i^{\prime}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\in\Phi\quad i^{\prime}\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}\quad i\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c},\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}\quad i^{\prime}\leq i\quad L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta}{L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}u^{\delta}\approx u^{\delta^{\prime}}:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

(by lookup)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta[\sigma]\vdash_{i^{\prime}}\sigma(u):T[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

(by lifting)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta[\sigma]\vdash_{i}\sigma(u):T[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}\delta[\sigma]\approx\delta^{\prime}[\sigma]:\Delta[\sigma]

(by equivalent local substitution lemma)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}\sigma(u)[\delta[\sigma]]\approx\sigma(u)[\delta^{\prime}[\sigma]]:T[\sigma][\delta[\sigma]]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

Notice that

T[\sigma][\delta[\sigma]]=T[\delta][\sigma]

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\omega$}\quad|\overrightarrow{\ell}|=|\overrightarrow{l}|=|\overrightarrow{l}^{\prime}|>0\quad\forall 0\leq n<|\overrightarrow{l}|~.~L\vdash\overrightarrow{l}(n)\approx\overrightarrow{l}^{\prime}(n):\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t~\$~\overrightarrow{l}\approx t^{\prime}~\$~\overrightarrow{l}^{\prime}:T[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]$}}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[\sigma]\approx t^{\prime}[\sigma]:\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}(T[\sigma])\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\omega$}

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{\textsc{m}}(t[\sigma])~\$~\overrightarrow{l}\approx(t^{\prime}[\sigma])~\$~\overrightarrow{l}^{\prime}:T[\sigma][\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]$}

Note that

T[\sigma][\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]=T[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}][\sigma[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]]=T[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}][\sigma]

because all
\overrightarrow{\ell}
do not occur in
\sigma
.

Case

\frac{G_{A}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad t=\texttt{box}\ \Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\quad s_{S}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ S):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\quad s_{T}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ T):\square((\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))}{L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{\Pi}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime},\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T},s_{S}/x_{S},s_{T}/x_{T}]\approx\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~(l\sqcup l^{\prime})$})):M_{\textsf{Typ}}[l\sqcup l^{\prime}/\ell,\Delta/g,t/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}

We first proceed by using IHs on the premises, which include the following
judgments:

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta[\sigma]

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta[\sigma]\vdash_{\textsc{c}}S[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta[\sigma],x:S[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

By using the same
\beta
rule, we must check the resulting left hand side and the
result type are equal to the target goal. Let us first consider the left hand side:

\displaystyle t_{\Pi}[\sigma,g/g,U_{S}^{\textsf{id}}/U_{S},U_{T}^{\textsf{id}}/U_{T}][l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime},\Delta[\sigma]/g,S[\sigma]/U_{S},T[\sigma]/U_{T},s_{S}[\sigma]/x_{S},s_{T}[\sigma]/x_{T}]

(by
Lemma

5.9
;
\ell
and
\ell^{\prime}
do not occur in
\sigma
)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle t_{\Pi}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime}][\sigma,g/g,U_{S}^{\textsf{id}}/U_{S},U_{T}^{\textsf{id}}/U_{T}][\Delta[\sigma]/g,S[\sigma]/U_{S},T[\sigma]/U_{T},s_{S}[\sigma]/x_{S},s_{T}[\sigma]/x_{T}]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle t_{\Pi}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime}][\sigma,\Delta[\sigma]/g,S[\sigma]/U_{S},T[\sigma]/U_{T}][s_{S}[\sigma]/x_{S},s_{T}[\sigma]/x_{T}]

(by naturality)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle t_{\Pi}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime}][\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T}][\sigma][s_{S}[\sigma]/x_{S},s_{T}[\sigma]/x_{T}]

(by
Lemma

5.9
)

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle t_{\Pi}[l/\ell,l^{\prime}/\ell^{\prime}][\Delta/g,S/U_{S},T/U_{T}][s_{S}/x_{S},s_{T}/x_{T}][\sigma]

Then we consider the result type in a similar way:

\displaystyle M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\sigma,g/g][l\sqcup l^{\prime}/\ell,\Delta[\sigma]/g,t[\sigma]/x_{T}]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle M_{\textsf{Typ}}[l\sqcup l^{\prime}/\ell][\sigma,g/g][\Delta[\sigma]/g,t[\sigma]/x_{T}]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle M_{\textsf{Typ}}[l\sqcup l^{\prime}/\ell][\Delta/g][\sigma][t[\sigma]/x_{T}]

\displaystyle=~

\displaystyle M_{\textsf{Typ}}[l\sqcup l^{\prime}/\ell][\Delta/g][t/x_{T}][\sigma]

Both equations allow us to conclude the goal.

∎

Next, we consider the effect of equivalent global substitutions on the judgments. We first define the equivalence relation between global substitutions:

\frac{L\vdash\Psi}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\cdot\approx\cdot:\cdot}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Phi\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Gamma\approx\Delta}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma,\Gamma/g\approx\sigma^{\prime},\Delta/g:\Phi,g:\textsf{Ctx}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Phi\quad L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Gamma\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad i\in\{\textsc{c},\textsc{d}\}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma,T/U\approx\sigma^{\prime},T^{\prime}/U:\Phi,u:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Phi\quad L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad i\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}t:T[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}:T[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma,t/u\approx\sigma^{\prime},t^{\prime}/u:\Phi,u:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})}

We can then consider similar properties of this equivalence relation.

Lemma 5.20 (Presupposition).

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Phi
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma^{\prime}:\Phi
.

Proof.

Induction.
∎

Lemma 5.21 (Equivalent Global Substitutions).

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
,
i\in\{\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Phi
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma[\sigma]\approx\Gamma[\sigma^{\prime}]
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
,
i\in\{\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Phi
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma[\sigma]\approx\Delta[\sigma^{\prime}]
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Phi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}T[\sigma]\approx T[\sigma^{\prime}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Phi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}T[\sigma]\approx T^{\prime}[\sigma^{\prime}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Phi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}t[\sigma]\approx t[\sigma^{\prime}]:T[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Phi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}t[\sigma]\approx t^{\prime}[\sigma^{\prime}]:T[\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Phi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}\delta[\sigma]\approx\delta[\sigma^{\prime}]:\Delta[\sigma]
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Phi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma[\sigma]\vdash_{i}\delta[\sigma]\approx\delta^{\prime}[\sigma^{\prime}]:\Delta[\sigma]
.

Proof.

We apply mutual induction. This lemma is much less sensitive to the exact statement of rules compared to

Lemma

5.15
. Since now we have proved the global substitution lemma, we could use conversion
rules whenever necessary.
∎

At this point, we have concluded that all substitutions are coherent with
well-formedness and typing judgments. Next, we shall move towards the full presupposition lemma and end our discussion on
syntactic properties with it.

5.3.
Context Equivalence and Presupposition

In order to establish presupposition, we must concern ourselves with the asymmetry in
the congruence rules of the equivalence judgments. Presupposition, intuitively, requires us to show that this asymmetry “does not
matter”. This intuition is formalized by the context equivalence lemma. In fact, we need two such lemmas, as we need to show one for local contexts and one
for global contexts. In light of that, let us proceed with the lemma for local contexts first.

Lemma 5.22 (Local Context Equivalence).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\delta:\Gamma^{\prime}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Gamma^{\prime}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We start by mutual induction. The base case is the local variable cases, where we simply apply the conversion rule
to the equivalence given by
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Delta\approx\Gamma
. We might also use presupposition (
Lemma

5.11
) to derive
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Delta
. Otherwise, most cases can be handled by IHs. In cases where local contexts are extended with variables, we shall carefully use
IHs to obtain the necessary premises to extend the equivalence of
\Delta
and

\Gamma
as well.

We consider a few cases:

Case

\frac{L\vdash l_{1}\approx l_{3}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l_{2}\approx l_{4}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{i}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{i}S\approx S^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\lambda^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S).t\approx\lambda^{l_{3},l_{4}}(x:S^{\prime}).t^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}}

In this case, the crucial part is to be able to invoke IH on
t\approx t^{\prime}
. We proceed as follows:

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

(by lifting and step case of the equivalence)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\approx\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

Then IHs will allow us to conclude the rest.

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Delta\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta,x_{T}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}:M[\texttt{box}\ U^{\textsf{id}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}[T/U]\approx\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M}~U\leftarrow((\texttt{box}\ T):\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}:M[\texttt{box}\ T/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

(by presupposition (
Lemma

5.11
))

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma

(note that well-formedness of
\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})
does not depend on

\Delta
or
\Gamma
)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Delta,x_{T}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\approx\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

(by global weakening)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Delta\approx\Gamma

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}:M[\texttt{box}\ U^{\textsf{id}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}[T/U]\approx\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M}~U\leftarrow((\texttt{box}\ T):\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}:M[\texttt{box}\ T/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

∎

As a corollary, we can prove the following lemma.

Lemma 5.23 (Symmetry and Transitivity of Local Contexts).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma_{1}\approx\Gamma_{2}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma_{2}\approx\Gamma_{3}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma_{1}\approx\Gamma_{3}
.

Proof.

Induction. Note that transitivity replies on the local context equivalence lemma.
∎

A similar lemma replaces the codomain local contexts of local substitutions. This variant is much simpler just by conversion rules.

Lemma 5.24 (Local Context Conversion).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Gamma^{\prime}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma^{\prime}\approx\Delta
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma^{\prime}\approx\Delta
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
.

Proof.

By induction. Propagate conversion rules together with the local substitution lemma in the step case.
∎

Then we work on the global context equivalence lemma. To state this lemma, we should first specify what does that mean for two global
contexts are equivalent.

\frac{}{L\vdash\cdot\approx\cdot}\par\frac{L\vdash\Psi\approx\Phi}{L\vdash\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx}\approx\Phi,g:\textsf{Ctx}}\par\frac{L\vdash\Psi\approx\Phi\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Gamma\approx\Delta\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad i\in\{\textsc{c},\textsc{d}\}}{L\vdash\Psi,U:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\approx\Phi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{i}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})}\par\frac{L\vdash\Psi\approx\Phi\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Gamma\approx\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad i\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}}{L\vdash\Psi,u:(\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\approx\Phi,u:(\Delta\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})}

Essentially, the equivalence of global contexts are just point-wise equivalence of
types within. We can reconstruct the well-formedness of both components from the premises:

Lemma 5.25 (Presupposition of Equivalence of Global Contexts).

If
L\vdash\Psi\approx\Phi
, then
L\vdash\Psi
and
L\vdash\Phi
.

For the global context equivalence lemma, we would like to take a shortcut by taking
advantage of the global substitution lemma.

Lemma 5.26.

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma\approx\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\textsf{id}:\Delta
.

Lemma 5.27.

If
L\vdash\Psi\approx\Phi
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\textsf{id}:\Phi
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction. In each step case, notice that weakening is used implicitly. Use
Lemma

5.26
to derive
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\textsf{id}:\Delta
whenever necessary.
∎

Lemma 5.28 (Global Context Equivalence).

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
and
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
and
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta
and
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
and
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
.

Proof.

We have
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\textsf{id}:\Phi
due to
Lemma

5.27
. Then by the global substitution lemma, we have our goal by knowing that a global
identity substitution
id
does no action.
∎

Finally, we prove the presupposition lemma, which is the last guiding lemma of the
layering principle.

Lemma 5.29 (Presupposition).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma
and
L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}
or
i=\textsc{m}\wedge l=\omega
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}
or
i=\textsc{m}\wedge l=\omega
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma
,

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}
or
i=\textsc{m}\wedge l=\omega
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

and
L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}
or
i=\textsc{m}\wedge l=\omega
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Delta
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma^{\prime}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Delta
.

Notice that in the statement of the lemma, we sometimes conclude
i=\textsc{m}\wedge l=\omega
. The only occasion when
\omega
is used is when universe polymorphic functions are
involved. In that case, we know for sure that
i=\textsc{m}
. In any other cases, we obtain
L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}
, which excludes
l=\omega
.

Proof.

We proceed by a mutual induction. In certain congruence rules, we must apply

Lemmas

5.15
and
5.21
to resolve the asymmetry in the rules. Otherwise, we simply apply the substitution lemmas whenever necessary. Note that our rules are stated with redundant premises to make sure this lemma
eventually checks out.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t~\$_{p}~T\approx t^{\prime}~\$_{p}~T^{\prime}:T^{\prime\prime}[T/U]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}~\$_{p}~T^{\prime}:T^{\prime\prime}[T^{\prime}/U]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

(by inversion)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

Notice that

T/U\approx T^{\prime}/U

We then have the goal by
Lemma

5.21
.
∎

5.4.
Coverage and Progress of Recursive Principles

Before moving to the semantics, let us pause a second and think about the recursive
principles: is it guaranteed to always pick a case from the branches? In this section, we would like to positively answer this question. The ingredient lies in the typing judgment at layer
c
and how the recursive
principle is formulated. Recall that the recursive principle for code of terms is

\frac{G_{A}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})):M_{\textsf{Trm}}[l^{\prime}/\ell,\Delta/g,T/U_{T},t/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}

In this rule, we see that the type of
t
is indexed by
l
,
\Delta
and
T
, both of
which live at layer
d
. When
t=\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime}
, then
t^{\prime}
must be typed at layer
c
. Then coverage is provided by the exhaustiveness of the branches which should enumerate
all possible types and terms at layer
c
. This is simple as we simply check the syntax at layer
c
and can confirm that the
branches are indeed exhaustive. Progress, on the other hand, requires both
\Delta
and
T
are in the right form
prescribed by the
\beta
rules. For example, the following rule gives the
\beta
rule for the
Nat
case as a term:

\frac{G_{A}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}_{\texttt{Nat}}[\Delta/g]\approx\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ \texttt{Nat}):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\texttt{Ty}_{0}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1$})):M_{\textsf{Trm}}[1/\ell,\Delta/g,\texttt{Ty}_{0}/U_{T},\texttt{box}\ \texttt{Nat}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}

where
T=\texttt{Ty}_{0}
is required. In this section, we show that when
t=\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime}
where
t^{\prime}
is of some concrete form
prescribed by a
\beta
rule, then the indices must have the right form.

We first consider the well-formed types at layer
c
:

Lemma 5.30.

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\vdash l\approx 0:\textsf{Level}
.

Proof.

By induction. The only applicable rules are the well-formedness rule and the
conversion rule. ∎

Similar lemmas can be stated and proved.

Lemma 5.31.

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime\prime}$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
and they are sub-derivations of the
assumption; moreover,
L\vdash l^{\prime\prime}\approx l\sqcup l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}
.

Proof.

Induction.
∎

That the judgments in the conclusion are sub-derivations ensures the well-foundedness
of the recursion. Effectively, the recursive principles recurse on the structures of the typing
derivations, so they are the most general principles that can be formulated on top the
syntax of MLTT.

Lemma 5.32.

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\texttt{El}^{l}~t\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:\texttt{Ty}_{l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}
as a
sub-derivation and
L\vdash l^{\prime}\approx l:\textsf{Level}
.

Lemma 5.33.

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\texttt{Ty}_{l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
, then
L\vdash l^{\prime}\approx 1+l:\textsf{Level}
.

Now we have exhausted all possible cases for types, so we move on to terms.

Lemma 5.34.

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
x:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\in\Gamma
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

and
L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}
.

The statement of lemmas for terms need to also consider the equivalence of types. The equivalence of types is implicitly handled by when evaluating the recursive
principles: since the equivalence is at layer
d
, computation applies, so the
equivalence can be acknowledged by the conversion checking algorithm.

Lemma 5.35.

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\texttt{Nat}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx\texttt{Ty}_{0}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\vdash l\approx 1:\textsf{Level}
.

Lemma 5.36.

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:s).t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime\prime}$}
, then as sub-derivations
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}s:\texttt{Ty}_{l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{El}^{l}~s\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:\texttt{Ty}_{l^{\prime}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l^{\prime}$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx\texttt{Ty}_{l\sqcup l^{\prime}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime\prime}$}
and
L\vdash l^{\prime\prime}\approx 1+{(l\sqcup l^{\prime})}:\textsf{Level}
.

Lemma 5.37.

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\texttt{Ty}_{l}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx\texttt{Ty}_{1+l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
and

L\vdash l^{\prime}\approx 2+l:\textsf{Level}
.

Lemma 5.38.

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\textsf{zero}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}

and
L\vdash l\approx 0:\textsf{Level}
.

Lemma 5.39.

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\textsf{succ}~t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then as a sub-derivation
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
,

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
and

L\vdash l\approx 0:\textsf{Level}
.

Lemma 5.40.

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
, then as sub-derivations

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}s:M[\textsf{zero}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$},y:M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}s^{\prime}:M[\textsf{succ}~x/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
;

moreover
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx M[t/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
and

L\vdash l^{\prime}\approx l:\textsf{Level}
.

Lemma 5.41.

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).t:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime\prime}$}
, then as sub-derivations

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
;

moreover
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T^{\prime}\approx\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime\prime}$}
and

L\vdash l^{\prime\prime}\approx l\sqcup l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}
.

Notice that in this case, we do not have the well-formedness of
T
as a
sub-derivation. This is reflected in the premises for the
t_{\lambda}
branch that the global variable
U_{T}

representing
T
lives at layer
d
. In general, a global assumption can live at layer
c
and have a recursive call only
if it has a sub-derivation in the typing judgment.

Lemma 5.42.

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}(t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~s:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime\prime}$}
, then as sub-derivations

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}s:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

moreover
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T^{\prime}\approx T[s/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime\prime}$}
and

L\vdash l^{\prime\prime}\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}
.

There is no other possible terms at layer
c
. These lemmas give us a syntactic account of coverage and progress of the recursive
principles. In the next section, we give a more rigorous semantic account.

6.
Reduction and Convertibility

We have finished syntactic verification for
DeLaM
. In this section, let us consider its dynamics by providing the reduction rules for
types and terms and the convertibility checking algorithm between two terms. The reduction relations to be given compute the weak head normal forms for
types and terms, respectively, and are sub-relations for equivalence judgments for
types and terms. We first give the syntax for weak head normal forms and neutral forms, and then give
the rules for reduction. We will need the reduction relations to write down the Kripke logical relations in the
next section as well as in the convertibility checking algorithm.

6.1.
Weak Head Normal Forms

The following gives the syntax for weak head normal forms and neutral forms for types
and terms. As usual, we use capital case for types and lower case for terms.

(Weak head normal form for types)

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ \texttt{Nat}\;|\;\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\;|\;\texttt{Ty}_{l}\;|\;\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T

\displaystyle\;|\;(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\;|\;(U:(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T\;|\;\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\;|\;\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})

(Neutral form for types)

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ U^{\delta}\;|\;\texttt{El}^{l}~\nu

(Weak head normal form for terms (
Nf
))

\displaystyle w

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ \nu\;|\;\texttt{Nat}\;|\;\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:s).t\;|\;\texttt{Ty}_{l}\;|\;\textsf{zero}\;|\;\textsf{succ}~t\;|\;\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).t

\displaystyle\;|\;\Lambda^{l}~{\overrightarrow{\ell}}.t\;|\;\Lambda^{l}~{g}.t\;|\;\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.t\;|\;\texttt{box}\ T\;|\;\texttt{box}\ t

(Neutral form for terms (
Ne
))

\displaystyle\nu

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\ x\;|\;u^{\delta}\;|\;\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~\nu\;|\;(\nu:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~s\;|\;\nu~\$~\overrightarrow{l}

\displaystyle\;|\;\nu~\$~\Gamma\;|\;\nu~\$_{p}~T\;|\;\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M}~U\leftarrow(\nu:\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}\;|\;\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{t}.M}~u\leftarrow(\nu:\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}

\displaystyle\;|\;\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(\nu:\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\;|\;\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ U^{\delta}):\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))

\displaystyle\;|\;\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(\nu:\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\;|\;\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ u^{\delta}):\square(\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))

Notice that for the recursive principles, we block on global variables, following

Hu and Pientka (2024a)
.

6.2.
Reduction Relations

There are two required reduction relations, one for types and one for terms. The one for types simply compute types from encodings via
El
. Unlike
Abel
et al. (2017)
who employed typed reductions, we deliberately
use untyped reductions and use preservation later to make sure the reductions are
well-defined. This deviation from
Abel
et al. (2017)
requires us to establish enough
syntactic theorems before hand. It is particularly important to use untyped reductions because of the way in which the logical relations
relate terms. We let
\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}
to be the reflexive transitive closure of
\rightsquigarrow
.

{{\texttt{El}^{0}~\texttt{Nat}}\rightsquigarrow\texttt{Nat}}\par{{\texttt{El}^{1+l}~\texttt{Ty}_{l}}\rightsquigarrow{\texttt{Ty}_{l}}}\par{{\texttt{El}^{l\sqcup l^{\prime}}~\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:s).t}\rightsquigarrow{\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:\texttt{El}^{l}~s).\texttt{El}^{l^{\prime}}~t}}\par\frac{t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}}{{\texttt{El}^{l}~t}\rightsquigarrow{\texttt{El}^{l}~t^{\prime}}}

The reduction rules for terms are simply the
\beta
equivalence rules.

{{\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~\textsf{zero}}\rightsquigarrow{s}}\par{{\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~(\textsf{succ}~t)}\rightsquigarrow{s^{\prime}[t/x,\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~t/y]}}\par{{(\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~s}\rightsquigarrow{t[s/x]}}\par{{(\Lambda^{l}~{\overrightarrow{\ell}}.t)~\$~\overrightarrow{l}}\rightsquigarrow{t[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]}}\par{{(\Lambda^{l}~{g}.t)~\$~\Delta}\rightsquigarrow{t[\Delta/g]}}\par{{(\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.t)~\$_{p}~T}\rightsquigarrow{t[T/U]}}\par{{\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M}~U\leftarrow((\texttt{box}\ T):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}}\rightsquigarrow{t^{\prime}[T/U]}}\par{{\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{t}.M}~u\leftarrow((\texttt{box}\ t):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~t^{\prime}}\rightsquigarrow{t^{\prime}[t/u]}}

The reduction rules for recursors follow the same principle. We write down only one rule as an example and omit the rest as they are just

\beta
rules:

{{\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ \texttt{Nat}):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}))}\rightsquigarrow{t_{\texttt{Nat}}[\Delta/g]}}

The congruence rules reduce the terms at the weak head positions to discover further
redices. There are at least one congruence rules for all elimination forms.

\frac{t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}}{{\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~t}\rightsquigarrow{\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~t^{\prime}}}\par\frac{t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}}{{(t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~s}\rightsquigarrow{(t^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~s}}

For elimination forms for meta-programming, we have

\frac{t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}}{{t~\$~\overrightarrow{l}}\rightsquigarrow{t^{\prime}~\$~\overrightarrow{l}}}\par\frac{t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}}{{t~\$~\Delta}\rightsquigarrow{t^{\prime}~\$~\Delta}}\par\frac{t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}}{{t~\$_{p}~T}\rightsquigarrow{t^{\prime}~\$_{p}~T}}\par\frac{t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}}{{\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M}~U\leftarrow(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~s}\rightsquigarrow{\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{T}.M}~U\leftarrow(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~s}}\par\frac{t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}}{{\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{t}.M}~u\leftarrow(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~s}\rightsquigarrow{\texttt{letbox}^{l^{\prime}}_{x_{t}.M}~u\leftarrow(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))~\textsf{in}~s}}

There are also congruence rules for the recursive principles. For the recursive principle for code of terms, we choose to reduce the type to weak
head normal form first and then reduce the term itself. This order is arbitrary and can be flipped. We simply fix a choice here.

\frac{t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}}{{\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))}\rightsquigarrow{\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))}}\par\frac{T\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}}{{\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))}\rightsquigarrow{\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))}}\par\frac{t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}}{{\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))}\rightsquigarrow{\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))}}

We first verify the fact that reductions are just sub-relations of the
equivalence judgments:

Lemma 6.1 (Soundness).

Given
i~\textsf{computable}
,

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
T\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Proof.

We proceed by mutual induction on the typing judgments and then invert the reduction
relations. We use
i~\textsf{computable}
to make sure computation rules are available. We select a few cases for discussion:

Case

The following is the only possible rule for types:

\frac{L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:\texttt{Ty}_{l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\texttt{El}^{l}~t\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

Inversion of
\texttt{El}^{l}~t\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}
gives four possible subcases. We only consider two:

Subcase

{\texttt{El}^{l_{1}\sqcup l_{2}}~\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:s).t}\rightsquigarrow{\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:\texttt{El}^{l_{1}}~s).\texttt{El}^{l_{2}}~t}

Then we know

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:s).t:\texttt{Ty}_{l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}

We further do an inner induction on the typing judgment above after generalizing

\texttt{Ty}_{l}
to some arbitrary
T
. There are only three cases to consider:

Subsubcase

\frac{L\vdash l_{1}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l_{2}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}s:\texttt{Ty}_{l_{1}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l_{1}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{El}^{l_{1}}~s\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\vdash_{i}t:\texttt{Ty}_{l_{2}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l_{2}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:s).t:\texttt{Ty}_{l_{1}\sqcup l_{2}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+{(l_{1}\sqcup l_{2})}$}}

Then this case we derive the goal immediately from the
El
rule for
\Pi

types.

Subsubcase
