\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:s).t:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:s).t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

In this case, we simply apply the inner IH to obtain the goal.

Subsubcase

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:s).t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:s).t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

Similarly, we use the inner IH to obtain the goal.

In general, when we know the form of a term, an inner induction must reveal only
three cases to consider. This pattern appears a lot when we consider cases for types.

Subcase

We have this case

\frac{t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}}{{\texttt{El}^{l}~t}\rightsquigarrow{\texttt{El}^{l}~t^{\prime}}}

Then by IH, we have

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}

We obtain the goal by the congruence rule for
El
.

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

By IH, we have

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

We obtain the goal by the conversion rule.

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

By IH, we have

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}

We obtain the goal by the conversion rule.

Case

For the recursive principle for natural numbers, there are three subcases after
inverting the reduction premise. We apply
\beta
rules or the congruence rule properly.

The same principle applies for the recursive principles for code, but a bit more
complex. We will use theorems from
Sec.

5.4
in combination of the congruence
rules to obtain our goals.

∎

As a corollary,

Lemma 6.2 (Preservation).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
T\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Proof.

We analyze
i
. If
i=\textsc{v}
, then there is no applicable reduction rule. If
i=\textsc{c}
, then we use lifting to lift
i
to
d
, then use the soundness,
presupposition and unlifting lemmas to obtain the goals. Otherwise, we use the soundness lemma and the presupposition lemma. ∎

The substitution lemmas require the well-formedness of types and the well-typedness of
terms to make use of algebraic laws of substitutions.

Lemma 6.3 (Universe Substitutions).

Given
i~\textsf{computable}
,

•

if
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
T\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}
and
L\vdash\phi:L^{\prime}
, then
T[\phi]\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}[\phi]
;

•

if
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}
and

L\vdash\phi:L^{\prime}
, then
t[\phi]\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}[\phi]
.

Lemma 6.4 (Local Substitutions).

Given
i~\textsf{computable}
,

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
T\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta
, then
T[\delta]\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}[\delta]
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta:\Delta
, then
t[\delta]\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}[\delta]
.

Lemma 6.5 (Global Substitutions).

Given
i~\textsf{computable}
,

•

if
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
T\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}
and
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi
, then
T[\sigma]\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}[\sigma]
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}
and

L\;|\;\Psi\vdash\sigma:\Phi
, then
t[\sigma]\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}[\sigma]
.

All lemmas above also work for the reflexive transitive closure versions of
reduction.

Lemma 6.6 (Determinacy).

•

If
T\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}
and
T\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}
, then
T^{\prime}=T^{\prime\prime}
.

•

If
t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}
and
t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime\prime}
, then
t^{\prime}=t^{\prime\prime}
.

If a multi-step reduction reaches a normal form, then we know this normal form is also
uniquely determined:

Lemma 6.7 (Determinacy).

•

If
T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W
and
T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W^{\prime}
, then
W=W^{\prime}
.

•

If
t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w
and
t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w^{\prime}
, then
w=w^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Induction. Use the fact that weak head normal forms do not reduce and determinacy of
single-step reduction.
∎

Due to preservation, we often are interested in keeping track of well-formedness and
well-typedness of types and terms. Therefore it is convenient to give the following convenient auxiliary judgments:

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad T\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\rightsquigarrow T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}T^{\prime}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\rightsquigarrow t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}t^{\prime}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

6.3.
Convertibility Checking

The convertibility checking is standard: we first reduce types or terms to their
weak head normal forms using reduction, and then recursively compare the
sub-structures. Either we detect a mismatch which causes a failure, or everything checks out and the
convertibility is verified.

Following this line, we give the following judgments for convertibility checking. Here we always quantify
i~\textsf{computable}
. The layering index
i
restricts only types (i.e. those in MLTT or in
DeLaM
), but
not terms. In other words, it is possible for convertibility checking to relate at layer
d
two
terms only well-typed at layer
m
, as long as these two terms have type well-formed
at layer
d
(i.e. MLTT). This is a critical property to establish a relation between the logical relations at
both layers.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
denotes that
T
and
T^{\prime}
are convertible at universe level
l
.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}W\Longleftrightarrow W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
denotes that
W
and
W^{\prime}
are convertible normal types.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}V\longleftrightarrow V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
denotes that
V
and
V^{\prime}
are convertible neutral types.

•

L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \Delta
denotes that
\Gamma
and
\Delta
are convertible contexts. This judgment is defined by using
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
pairwise.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
denotes that
t
and
t^{\prime}
of type
T
are convertible.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w\Longleftrightarrow w^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
denotes that
w
and
w^{\prime}
are convertible normal terms of a normal
type
W
.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
denotes that
\nu
and
\nu^{\prime}
are convertible
neutral terms.
T
is the result of inference.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
denotes that
\nu
and
\nu^{\prime}
are convertible neutral terms of
a normal type
W
.
W
is the result of inference.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta
denotes that
\delta
and
\delta^{\prime}
are convertible
local substitutions. This judgment is defined by using
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
pairwise.

We give the following convertibility checking rules for types first:

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad We propagate the convertibility for types pairwise to obtain the convertibility for local contexts.

\frac{L\vdash\Psi}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\cdot\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \cdot}\par\frac{L\vdash\Psi\quad g:\textsf{Ctx}\in\Psi}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}g\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ g}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \Delta,x:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

The convertibility of terms proceeds similarly. The following are checking rules that are available at both layers:

Notice here convertibility of
box
’ed types and terms are checked simply with
syntactic equality. The convertibility of neutral terms proceeds as follows. Similarly, we first give the checking rules that are available at both layers:

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\quad \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ u^{\delta^{\prime}}:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{i}M\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ M^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad When checking applications, we simply ignore the type inferred by checking
\nu
and

\nu^{\prime}
. This is fine because we already know
\nu
and
\nu^{\prime}
are well-typed so the type
annotations must be equivalent.

Then we give the rules only available at layer
m
:

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\omega$}\quad|\overrightarrow{\ell}|=|\overrightarrow{l}|=|\overrightarrow{l}^{\prime}|>\textsc{c}\quad\forall\textsc{c}\leq ’ed
global variables. To check the convertibility of neutral recursive principles, we recursively check the

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(\nu:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \textsf{elim}^{l_{3},l_{4}}~{\overrightarrow{M^{\prime}}}~\overrightarrow{b^{\prime}}~(\nu^{\prime}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})):M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\nu/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

We proceed by checking the convertibility of motives:

L\vdash l_{1}\approx l_{3}:\textsf{Level}\par L\vdash l_{2}\approx l_{4}:\textsf{Level}\par L,\ell\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M_{\textsf{Typ}}\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ M_{\textsf{Typ}}^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\par L,\ell\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx},U_{T}:(g\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$});\Gamma,x_{t}:\square(g\vdash_{\textsc{c}}U_{T}^{\textsf{id}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}M_{\textsf{Trm}}\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ M_{\textsf{Trm}}^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

We do the same for all the branches as well. Following the previous conventions, we group all these checking into
C_{A}
for

c
onvertibility checking for all premises. Then what we have left is to make sure the scrutinees are convertible.

\frac{C_{A}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \Delta^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(\nu:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \textsf{elim}^{l_{3},l_{4}}~{\overrightarrow{M^{\prime}}}~\overrightarrow{b^{\prime}}~(\nu^{\prime}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})):M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\nu/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}\par\frac{C_{A}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \Delta^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~(\nu:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \textsf{elim}^{l_{3},l_{4}}~{\overrightarrow{M^{\prime}}}~\overrightarrow{b^{\prime}}~(T^{\prime}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})){\nu^{\prime}}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[\nu/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}

If the scrutinees are
box
’ed global variables, then the check is always the same,
except that the global variables are compared syntactically:

\frac{C_{A}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \Delta^{\prime}\quad U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\in\Psi\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\delta:\Delta}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ U^{\delta}):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \textsf{elim}^{l_{3},l_{4}}~{\overrightarrow{M^{\prime}}}~\overrightarrow{b^{\prime}}~((\texttt{box}\ U^{\delta}):\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})):M_{\textsf{Typ}}[\texttt{box}\ U^{\delta}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}\par\frac{C_{A}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \Delta^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}\quad u:(\Delta\vdash_{i^{\prime}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\in\Psi\quad i^{\prime}\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\delta:\Delta}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\textsf{elim}^{l_{1},l_{2}}~{\overrightarrow{M}}~\overrightarrow{b}~((\texttt{box}\ u^{\delta}):\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \textsf{elim}^{l_{3},l_{4}}~{\overrightarrow{M^{\prime}}}~\overrightarrow{b^{\prime}}~(T^{\prime}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})){(\texttt{box}\ u^{\delta})}:M_{\textsf{Trm}}[\texttt{box}\ u^{\delta}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}

Now we have finished all the convertibility rules for neutral terms.

We simply let the convertibility for terms to propagate pairwise to derive the
convertibility for local substitutions:

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\quad\text{$\Gamma$ ends with $\cdot$}\quad|\Gamma|=\textsc{m}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\cdot^{\textsc{m}}\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \cdot^{\textsc{m}}:\cdot}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\quad g:\textsf{Ctx}\in\Psi\quad\text{$\Gamma$ ends with $g$}\quad|\Gamma|=\textsc{m}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\cdot_{g}^{\textsc{m}}\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \cdot_{g}^{\textsc{m}}:\cdot}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\quad g:\textsf{Ctx}\in\Psi\quad\quad\text{$\Gamma$ ends with $g$}\quad|\Gamma|=\textsc{m}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\textsf{wk}_{g}^{\textsc{m}}\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \textsf{wk}_{g}^{\textsc{m}}:g}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta\quad\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t^{\prime}:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta,t/x\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \delta^{\prime},t^{\prime}/x:\Delta,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

The convertibility algorithm is obtained by reading all the components for
convertibility rules as inputs and the neutral judgments consider types as outputs. If there is no corresponding rule, then two terms are not convertible; otherwise, two
terms are convertible. We verify some basic properties as follows:

Lemma 6.8 (Soundness).

Assuming
i~\textsf{computable}
,

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}W\Longleftrightarrow W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}W\approx W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}V\longleftrightarrow V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}V\approx V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w\Longleftrightarrow w^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w\approx w^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\approx\nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\approx\nu^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
.

Proof.

Mutual induction. Use
\eta
rules for all kinds of function types. Use congruence rules, presupposition and conversion rules when checking neutral
terms. ∎

Lemma 6.9.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}W\Longleftrightarrow W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}W\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w\Longleftrightarrow w^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ w^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Other lemmas like PER require the fundamental theorems so we postpone their proofs
until we have the semantic models.

7.
Logical Relations for
DeLaM

Previously, we have given the judgments of
DeLaM
, verified its syntactic
properties and given its reduction and convertibility algorithms. Starting this section, we establish the logical relation and prove the (weak)
normalization and convertibility properties of
DeLaM
. Following
Abel
et al. (2017)
, we proceeds as follows:

•

First we give a set of generic equivalence conditions for a parameterized
discussion of the logical relation.

•

Then we give the definition of the Kripke logical relations of types and
terms. The logical relations are parameterized by layers. In this step, we are only concerned about types that are available at all layers,
i.e. those in MLTT and unrelated to meta-programming.

•

Then we give the definition of the Kripke logical relations of local contexts
and local substitutions.

•

Then we branch off two orthogonal developments.

–

We give the definition of the Kripke logical relations of global contexts and
global substitutions.

–

We give the definition of the Kripke logical relations of types and terms,
again. But in this case, we must also give the definition for types that are related to
meta-programming, i.e. contextual types.

In fact, the definitions given by the two sub-steps above must consider each
other. Otherwise, we will not able to extend related global substitutions during the proof
of the fundamental theorems.

•

Next we give the semantic judgments. The semantic judgments require types, terms, etc. to be stable under all universe,
global and local substitutions.

•

Finally, we establish the fundamental theorems for the semantic judgments. Instantiating the generic equivalence gives us the proof of convertibility.

Due to layering, following
Sec.

3
, the generic equivalence, logical
relation and validity judgments are all layered. In fact, since computation exists at both layers
d
and
m
, the situation is very
complex.
Abel
et al. (2017)
instantiate their generic equivalence twice to obtain
the decidability of convertibility checking, and we will also be doing the same. Due to the complication of layering, our fundamental theorems must talk about all
layers. The difficulties of the logical relations lie in that how we can support code running
and recursions on code at the same time and justify them in the semantics.

7.1.
Generic Equivalence

Similar to
Sec.

3
, we first quantify four generic equivalence relations,
which will be instantiated to syntactic equivalence and convertibility later, and
their laws. This step provides modularity to logical relation argument: we simply instantiate this
generic equivalence to obtain different versions of the fundamental theorems. Due to dependent types, we define generic equivalence over
i\in\{\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}
:

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}V\sim V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
describes a generic type equivalence between two neutral
types at universe level
l
at layer
i
.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
describes a generic type equivalence between two types at
universe level
l
at layer
i
.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
describes a generic type equivalence between two neutral
terms of type
T
at universe level
l
at layer
i
.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
describes a generic type equivalence between two
terms of type
T
at universe level
l
at layer
i
.

From the four generic equivalence, we induce two equivalence of local contexts and
local substitutions by using the generic equivalence pairwise:

\frac{L\vdash\Psi}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\cdot\simeq\cdot}\par\frac{L\vdash\Psi\quad g:\textsf{Ctx}\in\Psi}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}g\simeq g}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\simeq\Delta\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\simeq\Delta,x:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\quad\text{$\Gamma$ ends with $\cdot$}\quad|\Gamma|=\textsc{m}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\cdot^{\textsc{m}}\simeq\cdot^{\textsc{m}}:\cdot}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\quad g:\textsf{Ctx}\in\Psi\quad\text{$\Gamma$ ends with $g$}\quad|\Gamma|=\textsc{m}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\cdot_{g}^{\textsc{m}}\simeq\cdot_{g}^{\textsc{m}}:\cdot}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\quad g:\textsf{Ctx}\in\Psi\quad\quad\text{$\Gamma$ ends with $g$}\quad|\Gamma|=\textsc{m}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\textsf{wk}_{g}^{\textsc{m}}\simeq\textsf{wk}_{g}^{\textsc{m}}:g}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\simeq\delta^{\prime}:\Delta\quad\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta,t/x\simeq\delta^{\prime},t^{\prime}/x:\Delta,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

The generic equivalence and the logical relations are invariant under all
weakenings. Therefore, we should make these notions clear here. Since there are three different contexts, we have three corresponding kinds of
weakenings. In particular,
\theta::L\Longrightarrow L^{\prime}
is the universe weakening. Following previous conventions,
\gamma::L\;|\;\Psi\Longrightarrow_{g}\theta
is a global
weakening, and
\tau::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Longrightarrow_{i}\Delta
is a local weakening. The subscript
i
denotes which layer the contexts
\Gamma
and
\Delta
live in. We can simultaneously weaken all three contexts at the same time. We simply apply
\theta
,
\gamma
and
\tau
in this order. We let
\psi
represent this triple:

\psi:=(\theta,\gamma,\tau)::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Longrightarrow_{i}L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta

where

\displaystyle\theta

\displaystyle::L\Longrightarrow L^{\prime}

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi[\theta]\Longrightarrow_{g}\Phi

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle::L^{\prime}\;|\;\theta;\Gamma[\theta][\gamma]\Longrightarrow_{i}\Delta

Similarly we let

\alpha:=(\theta,\gamma)::L\;|\;\Psi\Longrightarrow L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi

We can apply weakenings like substitutions to universe levels, types, terms, contexts
and substitutions as expected. The action is to shift the variables according to the specified weakenings. This is a standard action, despite having three separate notions, so we take it for
granted here. When it is clear from the context, we do not write down the weakening action at all to
avoid clutter.

Then we give the laws of the generic equivalence. Since the generic equivalence at layer
m
subsumes that at layer
d
, we first give
the laws that hold for both layers, and then incrementally add those that only hold at
layer
m
.

Law 7.1 (Subsumption).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}V\sim V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}V\simeq V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\simeq\nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

As a lemma, subsumption propagates to contexts and local substitutions:

Lemma 7.1 (Subsumption).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\simeq\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\simeq\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
.

Due to subsumption, we know that components in generic equivalence are well-formed or
well-typed:

Lemma 7.2 (Presupposition).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Proof.

By subsumption and presupposition.
∎

Law 7.2 (PER).

All four relations are PERs.

Law 7.3 (Type Conversion).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Law 7.4 (Context Equivalence).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}V\sim V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and

L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}V\sim V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and

L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and

L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and

L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Law 7.5 (Weakening).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}V\sim V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

\psi::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Longrightarrow_{i}L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta
, then

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}V\sim V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

\psi::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Longrightarrow_{i}L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta
, then

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

\psi::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Longrightarrow_{i}L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta
, then

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

\psi::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Longrightarrow_{i}L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta
, then

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Law 7.6 (Weak Head Closure).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}W\simeq W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w\simeq w^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Law 7.7 (Type Constructors).

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
,

•

if
L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\texttt{Ty}_{l}\simeq\texttt{Ty}_{l}^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\texttt{Ty}_{l}\simeq\texttt{Ty}_{l}^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{1+l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~2+l$}
;

•

then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\texttt{Nat}\simeq\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\texttt{Nat}\simeq\texttt{Nat}:\texttt{Ty}_{0}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1$}
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S\simeq S^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\simeq\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S^{\prime}).T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}s\simeq s^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{El}^{l}~s\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{l^{\prime}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l^{\prime}$}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:s).t\simeq\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:s^{\prime}).t^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{l\sqcup l^{\prime}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+{(l\sqcup l^{\prime})}$}
.

Law 7.8 (Neutral Types).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
,
U:(\Delta\vdash_{i^{\prime}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\in\Psi
,
i^{\prime}\in\{\textsc{c},\textsc{d}\}
,

i^{\prime}\leq i
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\simeq\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}U^{\delta}\sim U^{\delta^{\prime}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}
,
then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\texttt{El}^{l}~\nu\sim\texttt{El}^{l^{\prime}}~\nu^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Law 7.9 (Congruence).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\textsf{zero}\simeq\textsf{zero}:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\textsf{succ}~t\simeq\textsf{succ}~t^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}
,

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}(t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~x\simeq(t^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}
.

Law 7.10 (Congruence for Neutrals).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
and
x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\in\Gamma
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}x\sim x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
,
u:(\Delta\vdash_{i^{\prime}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\in\Psi
,

i^{\prime}\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}
,
i^{\prime}\leq i
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\simeq\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}u^{\delta}\sim u^{\delta^{\prime}}:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\vdash_{i}M\simeq M^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}s_{1}\simeq s_{3}:M[\textsf{zero}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$},y:M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}s_{2}\simeq s_{4}:M[\textsf{succ}~x/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s_{1}~(x,y.s_{2})~\nu\sim\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l^{\prime}}~(x.M^{\prime})~s_{3}~(x,y.s_{4})~\nu^{\prime}:M[\nu/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\vdash l_{1}\approx l_{3}:\textsf{Level}
,
L\vdash l_{2}\approx l_{4}:\textsf{Level}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S\simeq S^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}
,

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}
,

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S^{\prime\prime}).T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}\sqcup l_{2}$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}s\simeq s^{\prime}:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}(\nu:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S).T)~s\sim(\nu^{\prime}:\Pi^{l_{3},l_{4}}(x:S^{\prime}).T^{\prime})~s^{\prime}:T[s/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}
.

We derive that

Lemma 7.3 (Reflexivity of Local Identity Substitutions).

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}\simeq\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}:\Delta
.

Lemma 7.4 (Congruence of Global Variables).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
,
u:(\Delta\vdash_{i^{\prime}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\in\Psi
,
i^{\prime}\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}
and

i^{\prime}\leq i
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}u^{\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}}\sim u^{\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
,
U:(\Delta\vdash_{i^{\prime}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\in\Psi
,

i^{\prime}\in\{\textsc{c},\textsc{d}\}
and
i^{\prime}\leq i
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}U^{\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}}\sim U^{\textsf{id}_{\Gamma}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

At this point, we give the laws that should hold for both layers. Next, we consider laws that only hold at layer
i=\textsc{m}
. We first give the laws for type constructors.

Law 7.11 (Type Constructors).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma
,
L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

and
L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T\simeq\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\omega$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma
,
L\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\simeq(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma
,

.

.

Then we consider the law for neutral forms for recursive principles for code. The law follows a similar line to the equivalence judgments and the convertibility
checking: the evaluation is blocked when the scrutinee is neutral or is a
box
’ed
global variable.
