.

We conclude all the laws here fore the generic equivalence.

This generic equivalence will be instantiated twice times: the syntactic equivalence
judgments, and the convertibility checking judgments.
DeLaM
is way more complex than
Abel
et al. (2017)
’s work because

DeLaM
has computations at two layers,
d
and
m
. Therefore, we must derive the necessary properties from the fundamental theorems to be
proved shortly at each layer.

7.2.
Kripke Logical Relations for MLTT

The Kripke logical relations are parameterized by the generic equivalence. It is additionally indexed by another layering index
j\in\{\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}
, which
quantifies the types described by the relations. When
j=\textsc{d}
, we consider types from MLTT. When
j=\textsc{m}
, we consider all possible types. The reason for this distinction is to handle the lifting property from layer
c

(which has the terms as
d
) or
d
to

m
, where terms from MLTT are brought to
DeLaM
. On the semantic side, we need to make sure that terms from MLTT can interact with
“native” terms in
DeLaM
coherent. We further restrict
j=\textsc{d}
when
i=\textsc{d}
.

The Kripke logical relations are defined by

(1)

recursion on
j
, which effectively means the logical relations are
2-layered; also note when
i=\textsc{d}
,
j=\textsc{d}
is determined;

(2)

a transfinite well-founded recursion on the universe levels, and

(3)

induction-recursion on related types and terms.

In particular, the recursion on
j
is necessary, as the relations when
j=\textsc{m}
depend
on the validity judgments of
j=\textsc{d}
. When we do a recursion on universe levels, we must mind the well-foundedness of
universe levels. As we have discussed in
Sec.

4.3
, we are sure that all universe
levels must find a finite number of steps to descend to
0
. The only problem is
\omega
, which is not finite. Thus we must include one large cardinal to handle this level, hence the transfinite
recursion. Luckily, we do not have to think about it most of the time as we cannot really use

\omega
to do anything special at all. Note that our relations do not exactly follow
Abel
et al. (2017)
tightly,
where two relations are defined for types and terms respectively. In our case, we provide simpler inductive-recursive definitions, where only one
relation is defined for types and for terms respectively. This style is more akin to the PER models in untyped domains by

Abel (2013)
;
Hu
et al. (2023)
, except that our logical
relations are Kripke. We follow a proof schema that combines that of
Abel
et al. (2017)
and that
of
Abel (2013)
;
Hu
et al. (2023)
. We define the following judgments:

•

\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
denotes that two types
T
and
T^{\prime}
are related. This relation is defined inductively. We use
\mathcal{D}
to mark give a name to the derivation as we will do recursion on it.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
denotes that two terms
t
and
T^{\prime}
related by
\mathcal{D}
. This relation is defined by a recursion on
\mathcal{D}
.

•

\mathcal{E}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\Gamma\approx\Delta
denotes that two contexts are related. It is a generalization of
\mathcal{D}
.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{E}
denotes that two local substitutions

\delta
and
\delta^{\prime}
are related. It is a generalization of
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
by doing recursion on
\mathcal{E}
.

For convenience, we define the following:

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

\displaystyle:=L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T\approx T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

\displaystyle:=\text{for some $T^{\prime}$, }\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\text{ and }L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

\displaystyle:=L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t\approx t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\Gamma

\displaystyle:=L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\Gamma\approx\Gamma

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta

\displaystyle:=\text{for some $\Delta^{\prime}$, }\mathcal{E}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}\text{ and }L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\delta:\Delta

\displaystyle:=L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\delta\approx\delta:\Delta

Now we proceed to define the relations. We begin with the natural numbers.

\mathcal{D}::\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}

Then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
is defined by
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}
, which we
define as follows:

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w\simeq w^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}w\simeq w^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}}\par\frac{}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}\textsf{zero}\simeq\textsf{zero}:\texttt{Nat}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}\textsf{succ}~t\simeq\textsf{succ}~t^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}\nu\simeq\nu^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}}

Then we consider universes.

\mathcal{D}::\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\texttt{Ty}_{l_{1}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l_{1}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\texttt{Ty}_{l_{2}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l_{2}$}\quad\quad L\vdash l_{1}\approx l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l_{2}\approx l:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}}

Then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
is defined by

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w:\texttt{Ty}_{l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w\simeq w^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}
, which means that
t
and
t^{\prime}
are equivalent
types at level
l
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\texttt{El}^{l}~w\approx\texttt{El}^{l}~w^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, which means that the corresponding
types of
w
and
w^{\prime}
are related.

The last condition requires the well-founded recursion on the universe levels in order
to refer back to the relation for types. Notice that the universe level decreases by one so this definition is valid.

Then we define the relation for
\Pi
types.

\mathcal{D}::\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S_{1}).T_{1}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S_{2}).T_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S_{1}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S_{1}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}T_{1}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}T_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S_{1}).T_{1}\simeq\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S_{2}).T_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}\quad\mathcal{E}::(\forall~\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}S_{1}\approx S_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\quad\mathcal{F}::(\forall~\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\text{ and }L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}s\approx s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{E}(\psi))~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T_{1}[s/x]\approx T_{2}[s^{\prime}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}}

This case is particularly complex. Let us digest the premises one by one. First, we require that both types
T
and
T^{\prime}
to reduce to some
\Pi
types. The typing judgments require the components of the
\Pi
types are well-formed. Further, the
\Pi
types themselves are equivalent. Then
\mathcal{E}
requires that
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
are related under any weakening. This makes the relation Kripke.
\mathcal{F}
is similar but require
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
remain related given any two related terms

s
and
s^{\prime}
. Furthermore, in reality, we should put down sufficient premises for equivalent
universes to allow syntactically different universes to appear in both
T
and
T^{\prime}
,
but due to the size of this rule, we only use
l
and
l^{\prime}
here. We apply the same principle in other rules in the rest of this technical report.

Then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
is defined by

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S_{1}).T_{1}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S_{2}).T_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w\simeq w^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S_{1}).T_{1}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w\simeq w^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S_{2}).T_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}
, which is duplicated to
make symmetry a simpler property,

•

for all
\forall\ \psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma
and
\mathcal{A}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}s\approx s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{E}(\psi))
, moreover, we add
equivalence assumptions to remove the effect of type annotations,

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}S_{1}\simeq S_{1}^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}S_{2}\simeq S_{2}^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:S_{1}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}T_{1}\simeq T_{1}^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
,

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:S_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}T_{2}\simeq T_{2}^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
, then
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}(w:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S_{1}^{\prime}).T_{1}^{\prime})~s\approx(w^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S_{2}^{\prime}).T_{2}^{\prime})~s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{F}(\psi,\mathcal{A}))
.

The next case is neutral types.

\mathcal{D}::\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}V\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}V\sim V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

Then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
is defined by

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\nu:V\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\nu^{\prime}:V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:V\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Again, we duplicate
\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}
to make symmetry easy.

The last case possible for both layers is equivalence of universe levels. This case is introduced for bureaucratic purposes and often ignored.

\mathcal{D}::\frac{\mathcal{E}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

Then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
is defined by
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{E})
.

Now we have given all logical relations for types and terms that are available at both layers. In order to give the logical relations for layer
m
, we need to first give the
logical relations for local contexts and local substitutions, as the semantics of
contextual types depend on them. The complexity of our logical relations primarily comes from the fact that we must be
able to handle computation at two layers (
d
and
m
) and different lifting
behaviors.

We proceed by defining related local contexts inductively and then the corresponding
recursive case for local substitutions.

\mathcal{E}::\frac{L\vdash\Psi}{L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\cdot\approx\cdot}

Then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{E}
is defined by
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
and then checking
\Gamma
:

•

if
\Gamma
ends with
\cdot
, then
\delta=\delta^{\prime}=\cdot^{|\Gamma|}
;

•

if
\Gamma
ends with
g
, then
g:\textsf{Ctx}\in\Psi
and
\delta=\delta^{\prime}=\cdot^{|\Gamma|}_{g}
;

\mathcal{E}::\frac{L\vdash\Psi\quad g:\textsf{Ctx}\in\Psi}{L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}g\approx g}

Then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{E}
is defined as
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma
and

•

\Gamma
also ends with
g
,

•

\delta=\delta^{\prime}=\textsf{wk}^{|\Gamma|}_{g}
.

\mathcal{E}::\frac{\mathcal{F}::\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}\quad\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\Delta,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\approx\Delta^{\prime},x:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

Then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{E}
is defined as

•

\delta=\delta_{1},t/x
,

•

\delta^{\prime}=\delta_{1}^{\prime},t^{\prime}/x
,

•

\mathcal{C}::\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{F}(\alpha)
,

•

\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D}(\alpha,\mathcal{C}(\alpha)))
.

where we let
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
to be

\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi\text{ and }L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{F}(\alpha)~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T[\delta]\approx T^{\prime}[\delta^{\prime}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

The judgment
L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
requires the stability under local substitutions
of the relation between
T
and
T^{\prime}
. We apply the same principle to related terms and derive the judgment

L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
, which is given by

\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi\text{ and }\mathcal{C}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{F}(\alpha)~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}t[\delta]\approx t^{\prime}[\delta^{\prime}]:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D}(\alpha,\mathcal{C}))

7.3.
Properties for Logical Relations When
j=\textsc{d}

In this section, we pause our progress of defining the logical relations when
j=\textsc{m}
. Following previous lines of work, when we give the Kripke logical relations to
contextual types, we will have to refer to the validity judgments of types and terms
in the logical relations. Therefore, in this section, we first work out a list of properties of the logical
relations when
j=\textsc{d}
, and then in the next section, we define semantic judgments for
global contexts and global substitutions, and then validity judgments. Once we have the validity judgments when
j=\textsc{d}
, we can then finish writing down the
logical relations for contextual types. Without further ado, let us start proving some lemmas. The list of lemmas mainly follows
Abel (2013)
;
Hu
et al. (2023)

though we also take
Abel
et al. (2017)
into consideration.

Lemma 7.5 (Weakening).

•

If
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma
, then

\mathcal{E}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
and

\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma
, then

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t^{\prime}\approx t:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

Induction on
\mathcal{D}
. Note that typing judgments are invariant under weakenings. Also use the weakening law for generic equivalence. ∎

Lemma 7.6 (Escape).

•

If
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Proof.

Induction on
\mathcal{D}
. Use the subsumption law of the generic equivalence. ∎

Lemma 7.7 (Reflexivity of Neutral).

If
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\nu\approx\nu^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
.

Proof.

Induction on
\mathcal{D}
. Use the conversion and subsumption laws of generic equivalence and then the
soundness of multi-step reductions and the escape lemma to obtain the equivalence
between
\nu
and
\nu^{\prime}
as normal forms. In the function case, we use the congruence for neutral law of generic equivalence
to show that the results of applying two neutral function are related.
∎

Lemma 7.8 (Weak Head Expansion).

•

If
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T_{1}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T_{1}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T_{1}\approx T_{1}^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t_{1}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t_{1}^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t_{1}\approx t_{1}^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
.

Proof.

Induction on
\mathcal{D}
. Use transitivity of reductions.
∎

Lemma 7.9 (Symmetry).

•

If
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
\mathcal{E}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T^{\prime}\approx T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t^{\prime}\approx t:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

We do induction on
\mathcal{D}
. Since our definition is designed with symmetry in mind, symmetry is rather immediate.
∎

Lemma 7.10 (Right Irrelevance).

If
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
\mathcal{E}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

We do induction on
\mathcal{D}
and then invert
\mathcal{E}
. We consider the function case. In this case, we have premises

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}

\displaystyle::(\forall~\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}S_{1}\approx S_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$})

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2}

\displaystyle::(\forall~\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma~.~\forall~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}s\approx s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D}_{1}(\psi))~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T_{1}[s/x]\approx T_{2}[s^{\prime}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}

\displaystyle::(\forall~\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}S_{1}\approx S_{2}^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$})

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}

\displaystyle::(\forall~\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma~.~\forall~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}s\approx s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{E}_{1}(\psi))~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T_{1}[s/x]\approx T_{2}^{\prime}[s^{\prime}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})

from
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{E}
. Here
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
are the premises of
\mathcal{D}
, and likewise for
\mathcal{E}
. By determinacy, we know that

T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{1}).T_{1}

must be unique. Moreover, we also know

\displaystyle T^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{2}).T_{2}

\displaystyle T^{\prime\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{2}^{\prime}).T_{2}^{\prime}

The most difficult part is to show that given

•

\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma
,

•

\mathcal{A}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}s\approx s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{E}_{1}(\psi))

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}S_{1}\simeq S_{3}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}S_{2}\simeq S_{4}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:S_{1}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\vdash_{i}T_{1}\simeq T_{3}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}
, and

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:S_{2}^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\vdash_{i}T_{2}^{\prime}\simeq T_{4}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}
,

then

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}(w:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{3}).T_{3})~s\approx(w^{\prime}:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{4}).T_{4})~s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{E}_{2}(\psi,\mathcal{A}))

From
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
, together with

•

\mathcal{B}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}s\approx s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D}_{1}(\psi))
by IH,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:S_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\vdash_{i}T_{2}\simeq T_{4}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}
, where we
know
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S_{2}\approx S_{2}^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}
by escape, the subsumption law of generic
equivalence, transitivity and local context equivalence of syntactic and generic
equivalence.

we have

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}(w:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{3}).T_{3})~s\approx(w^{\prime}:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{4}).T_{4})~s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D}_{2}(\psi,\mathcal{B}))

By another IH, we have the goal.
∎

Lemma 7.11 (Left Irrelevance).

If
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T^{\prime}\approx T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
\mathcal{E}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T^{\prime\prime}\approx T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D})
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{E})
.

Proof.

Immediate by symmetry and right irrelevance.
∎

The left and right irrelevance lemmas are called the irrelevance lemma. It says that the exact relation between types is not important as long as their normal
forms are related.

Lemma 7.12 (Reflexivity and Transitivity).

•

If
\mathcal{D}_{1}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T_{1}\approx T_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T_{2}\approx T_{3}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
\mathcal{D}_{3}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T_{1}\approx T_{3}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
\mathcal{E}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T_{1}\approx T_{1}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t_{1}\approx t_{2}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D}_{1})
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t_{2}\approx t_{3}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D}_{2})
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t_{1}\approx t_{3}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D}_{3})
.

•

\mathcal{F}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T_{1}\approx T_{1}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t_{1}\approx t_{2}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D}_{1})
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t_{1}\approx t_{1}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{F})
.

Proof.

We do induction on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and then invert
\mathcal{D}_{2}
. The function case is the most complex one. We have the following premises:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{1}

\displaystyle::(\forall~\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}S_{1}\approx S_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$})

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{2}

\displaystyle::(\forall~\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma~.~\forall~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}s\approx s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{A}_{1}(\psi))~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}S_{1}^{\prime}[s/x]\approx S_{2}^{\prime}[s^{\prime}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{1}

\displaystyle::(\forall~\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}S_{2}\approx S_{3}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$})

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{2}

\displaystyle::(\forall~\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma~.~\forall~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}s\approx s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{E}_{1}(\psi))~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}S_{2}^{\prime}[s/x]\approx S_{3}^{\prime}[s^{\prime}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})

from
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{E}
. Here
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{A}_{2}
are the premises of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
, and
\mathcal{B}_{1}
and
\mathcal{B}_{2}
are from
\mathcal{D}_{2}
. By determinacy, we know that

\displaystyle T_{1}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{1}).S_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle T_{2}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{2}).S_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle T_{3}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{3}).S_{3}^{\prime}

Now, we should construct the transitivity for types and terms at the same time to
understand how this proof is going to check out. First we let

\mathcal{C}_{1}::(\forall~\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}S_{1}\approx S_{3}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$})

be the result of IH on
\mathcal{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{B}_{1}
. Then our goal is to show that

The most difficult part is to show that given

•

\psi::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\Longrightarrow_{i}L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma
,

•

\mathcal{F}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}s\approx s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{C}_{1}(\psi))

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}S_{1}\simeq S_{4}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}S_{3}\simeq S_{5}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:S_{1}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\vdash_{i}S_{1}^{\prime}\simeq S_{4}^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}
, and

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:S_{3}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\vdash_{i}S_{3}^{\prime}\simeq S_{5}^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}
,

then

•

\mathcal{C}_{2}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}S_{1}^{\prime}[s/x]\approx S_{3}^{\prime}[s^{\prime}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}(w:\Pi^{{}_{,}1}(l_{1}:l_{2}).x)~S_{4}{S_{4}^{\prime}}s\approx(w_{3}:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{5}).S_{5}^{\prime})~s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{C}_{2})

where
t_{k}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w_{k}
for
k\in\{1,2,3\}
.

Our plan is the following:

(1)

We first relate
S_{1}^{\prime}[s/x]
and
S_{2}^{\prime}[s/x]
, resp.

(w_{1}:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{4}).S_{4}^{\prime})~s
and
(w_{2}:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{4}).S_{4}^{\prime})~s
, which
requires

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}s\approx s:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{A}_{1}(\psi))

which is derived from reflexivity and irrelevance;

(2)

and then relate
S_{2}^{\prime}[s/x]
and
S_{3}^{\prime}[s/x]
, resp.

(w_{2}:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{4}).S_{4}^{\prime})~s
and
(w_{2}:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S_{5}).S_{5}^{\prime})~s^{\prime}
,
which requires

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}s\approx s^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{B}_{1}(\psi))

which is immediate from irrelevance.

The final missing piece is reflexivity. For types, it is just a result from transitivity and symmetry. For terms, it is reflexivity and transitivity of related types and then
irrelevance.

This concludes the function case.
∎

Next, we work on the properties for related local contexts and local substitutions. We first consider the built property of weakening. Weakening can be seen as a case of the Yoneda lemma, where we only depend on
composition of weakenings.

Lemma 7.13 (Weakening).

•

If
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
and
\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi
, then
\mathcal{E}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}
,
\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi

and
\tau::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma^{\prime}\Longrightarrow_{i}\Gamma
, then

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{E}
.

Proof.

Induction on
\mathcal{D}
. In the two base cases, we know
L^{\prime}\vdash\Phi
by weakening. In the step case, we simply store the given weakenings in a composition. ∎

Notice that the second statement is a little bit strengthened. It is possible due to local weakening of related types and terms.

We should first prove the reflexivity between identity local substitutions before
proving the the escape lemma.

Lemma 7.14 (Reflexive Local Weakenings).

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Delta,\Gamma\approx\Delta^{\prime},\Gamma
and
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta,\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\textsf{wk}^{|\Gamma|}_{\Delta}\approx\textsf{wk}^{|\Gamma|}_{\Delta}:\mathcal{D}
.

Proof.

We do induction on
\mathcal{D}
and consider the step case

\mathcal{D}::\frac{\mathcal{E}::\forall\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}\quad\mathcal{F}::L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{E}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\approx\Delta^{\prime},x:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

(by IH)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}::

\displaystyle\forall\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$},\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\textsf{wk}^{1+|\Gamma|}_{\Delta}\approx\textsf{wk}^{1+|\Gamma|}_{\Delta}:\mathcal{E}(\alpha)

(by assumption)

\displaystyle\alpha::

\displaystyle L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi

(as
\mathcal{F}(\alpha,\mathcal{B}(\alpha))
)

\displaystyle L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$},\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T[\textsf{wk}^{1+|\Gamma|}_{\Delta}]\approx T^{\prime}[\textsf{wk}^{1+|\Gamma|}_{\Delta}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}::

\displaystyle L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$},\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

(by weakening and presupposition)

\displaystyle L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Gamma,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$},\Delta

(by congruence for neutrals of generic equivalence)

\displaystyle L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$},\Gamma\vdash_{i}x\sim x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

(by reflexivity of neutral)

\displaystyle L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$},\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}x\approx x:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$},\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\textsf{wk}^{1+|\Gamma|}_{\Delta},x/x\approx\textsf{wk}^{1+|\Gamma|}_{\Delta},x/x:\mathcal{D}

Thus we conclude the goal.
∎

Corollary 7.15 (Reflexive Local Identity Substitutions).

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\approx\Delta
and
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Gamma\approx\Delta
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\textsf{id}\approx\textsf{id}:\mathcal{D}
.

Proof.

This is a specialization of the previous lemma.
∎

Knowing all local identity substitutions are reflexively related, we can then prove
the escape lemma.

Lemma 7.16 (Escape).

•

If
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1}\approx\Delta_{2}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Delta_{1}\simeq\Delta_{2}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\simeq\delta^{\prime}:\Delta_{1}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\simeq\delta^{\prime}:\Delta_{2}
.

Proof.

Induction on
\mathcal{D}
. We consider the step case.

\mathcal{D}::\frac{\mathcal{E}::\forall\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1}\approx\Delta_{2}\quad\mathcal{F}::L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{E}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1},x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\approx\Delta_{2},x:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Delta_{1}\simeq\Delta_{2}

(by
Corollary

7.15
)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\textsf{id}\approx\textsf{id}:\mathcal{E}(\textsf{id})

(as
\mathcal{F}(\textsf{id},\mathcal{E}(\textsf{id}))
)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

(by escape)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta_{1}\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

Hence we conclude the first statement.

In the second statement, we have
\delta=\delta_{1},t/x
and
\delta^{\prime}=\delta_{1}^{\prime},t^{\prime}/x
, then

(by assumption)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{1}^{\prime}:\mathcal{E}(\textsf{id})

(by IH)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta_{1}\simeq\delta_{1}^{\prime}:\Delta_{1}\text{ and }L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta_{1}\simeq\delta_{1}^{\prime}:\Delta_{2}

(by assumption)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{F}(\textsf{id},\mathcal{E}(\textsf{id})))

(by escape of related terms)

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T[\delta_{1}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

Therefore we conclude the second statement as well.
∎

Lemma 7.17 (Symmetry).

•

If
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1}\approx\Delta_{2}
, then
\mathcal{E}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{2}\approx\Delta_{1}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta^{\prime}\approx\delta:\mathcal{E}
.

Proof.

Induction on
\mathcal{D}
. Use the symmetry of related types to obtain the goal.
∎

Lemma 7.18 (Right Irrelevance).

If
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1}\approx\Delta_{2}
,
\mathcal{E}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1}\approx\Delta_{3}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{E}
.

Proof.

Do induction on
\mathcal{D}
and then invert
\mathcal{E}
. Use the right irrelevance of related terms. ∎

Lemma 7.19 (Left Irrelevance).

If
\mathcal{D}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1}\approx\Delta_{2}
,
\mathcal{E}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{3}\approx\Delta_{1}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{D}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{E}
.

Proof.

A direct consequence of right irrelevance and symmetry.
∎

Lemma 7.20 (Reflexivity and Transitivity).

•

If
\mathcal{D}_{1}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1}\approx\Delta_{2}
and

\mathcal{D}_{2}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{2}\approx\Delta_{3}
, then

\mathcal{D}_{3}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1}\approx\Delta_{3}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{2}:\mathcal{D}_{1}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta_{2}\approx\delta_{3}:\mathcal{D}_{2}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{3}:\mathcal{D}_{3}
.

•

\mathcal{E}::L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1}\approx\Delta_{1}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{2}:\mathcal{D}_{1}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{1}:\mathcal{E}
.

Proof.

Do induction on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and then invert
\mathcal{D}_{2}
. The proof proceeds very similarly to the relations for types and terms. We only consider the step case. In this case, we have the following premises:

•

\mathcal{F}_{1}::\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1}^{\prime}\approx\Delta_{2}^{\prime}
,

•

\mathcal{F}_{2}::\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{2}^{\prime}\approx\Delta_{3}^{\prime}
,

•

\mathcal{A}_{1}::L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T_{1}\approx T_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

\mathcal{A}_{2}::L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}_{2}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T_{2}\approx T_{3}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}_{2}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T^{\prime}\approx T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}_{3}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t_{1}\approx t_{2}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{A}_{1}(\alpha,\mathcal{F}_{1}(\alpha)))
,

•

\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t_{2}\approx t_{3}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{A}_{2}(\alpha,\mathcal{F}_{2}(\alpha)))
,

By IH, it is easy to show

\mathcal{F}_{3}::\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1}^{\prime}\approx\Delta_{3}^{\prime}

The difficult goals are

•

\mathcal{A}_{3}::L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}_{3}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T_{1}\approx T_{3}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, and

•

\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t_{1}\approx t_{3}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{A}_{3}(\alpha,\mathcal{F}_{3}(\alpha)))
.

We first assume
\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi
. To prove the first statement, we further assume
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{j}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\mathcal{F}_{3}(\alpha)
. Then we do a similar reasoning to the function case for transitivity of related
types.

(1)

We first relate
T_{1}[\delta]
and
T_{2}[\delta]
by using reflexivity.

(2)

Then we related
T_{2}[\delta]
and
T_{3}[\delta^{\prime}]
.

(3)

Then we apply transitivity of related types.

To prove the second statement, we can simply use irrelevance so that transitivity
can eventually apply on
\mathcal{A}_{3}(\alpha,\mathcal{F}_{3}(\alpha))
.
∎

Lemma 7.21 (Transitivity).

Given

•

\mathcal{F}_{1}::\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1}\approx\Delta_{2}
,

•

\mathcal{F}_{2}::\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{2}\approx\Delta_{3}
, and

•

\mathcal{F}_{3}::\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta_{1}\approx\Delta_{3}
,

then

•

if
\mathcal{D}_{1}:e:L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}::L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}_{2}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T^{\prime}\approx T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then

\mathcal{D}_{3}::L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}_{3}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D}_{1})
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}_{2}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t^{\prime}\approx t^{\prime\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D}_{3})
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\mathcal{F}_{3}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime\prime}:\texttt{El}(\mathcal{D}_{3})
.

Proof.

The first statement is what we have proved in transitivity previously. The second statement uses the transitivity of related terms. ∎

7.4.
Semantic Well-formedness of Global Contexts and Related Global Substitutions

Following previous lines of work, when we give the Kripke logical relations to
contextual types, we will have to refer to related types and terms in the logical
relations under some invariants. Therefore, as early as it might seem, we must consider the semantics for global
contexts and global substitutions to see what it needs to learn how exactly the
semantics of contextual types can be defined. We then describe the semantics of global contexts and global substitutions, similar to
that of local contexts and local substitutions, as inductive-recursive definitions. Types and terms in a global substitution consist of two components:

(1)

the use of logical relations showing related terms having related computation,

(2)

together with the maintenance of syntactic structures, if the type or the term
comes from layer
v
or
c
.

To handle the first component, we define several auxiliary definitions. These definitions essentially state that logical relations should remain stable under
weakenings and local substitutions. We give the definitions as follows. We define
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
to be given

•

\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi
,

•

k\geq\textsc{d}
, and

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{k}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma
,

it holds that

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{k}^{\textsc{d}}T[\delta]\approx T^{\prime}[\delta^{\prime}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

Similarly, we define
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
to be given

•

\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi
,

•

k\geq\textsc{d}
, and

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{k}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma
,

we have

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{k}^{\textsc{d}}t[\delta]\approx t^{\prime}[\delta^{\prime}]:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

We also need a similar relation for local contexts and local substitutions.
L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
is defined as

\forall~\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi\text{ and }k\geq\textsc{d}~.~L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{k}^{j}\Delta\approx\Delta

We define
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
as

•

\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi
,

•

k\geq\textsc{d}
, and

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{k}^{\textsc{d}}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{1}^{\prime}:\Gamma
,

we have

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{k}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\circ\delta_{1}\approx\delta^{\prime}\circ\delta_{1}:\Delta

We define their non-relational version:

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

\displaystyle:=L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

\displaystyle:=L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta

\displaystyle:=L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta

\displaystyle L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\delta:\Delta

\displaystyle:=L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta:\Delta

These would have been the semantic judgments for the fundamental theorems for MLTT. However, they are not enough if we want to establish the fundamental theorems for the
whole
DeLaM
. That is because the semantic judgments for
DeLaM
require stability under all
sorts of substitutions, including universe, global and local substitutions. Thus, in this section, we will define when a global context and global
substitutions are semantically well-formed in order to state the semantic judgments.

These definitions are closed under weakenings and local substitutions by design:

Lemma 7.22 (Weakenings).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi
,
then
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi
,
then
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
and
\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi
, then
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
and
\alpha::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\Longrightarrow L\;|\;\Psi
, then
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
.

Lemma 7.23 (Local Substitutions).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma
,
then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T[\delta]\approx T^{\prime}[\delta^{\prime}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma
,
then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}t[\delta]\approx t^{\prime}[\delta^{\prime}]:T[\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\delta_{1}\approx\delta_{1}^{\prime}:\Gamma
, then

L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\delta\circ\delta_{1}\approx\delta^{\prime}\circ\delta_{1}^{\prime}:\Delta
.

Lemma 7.24 (Local Weakenings).

If
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta,\Gamma\approx\Delta^{\prime},\Gamma
and
L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta,\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq i}^{j}\textsf{wk}^{|\Gamma|}_{\Delta}\approx\textsf{wk}^{|\Gamma|}_{\Delta}:\Delta
.

Proof.

Notice that a local weakening only shorten a well-formed local substitution.
∎

Lemma 7.25 (Semantic Conversion).

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Lemma 7.26 (PER).

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

L\;|\;\Psi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq i}^{j}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Delta
are
PERs.

Next, we handle the second component to record the syntactic information of types and terms. Following
Hu and Pientka (2024a)
;
Hu and Pientka (2024b)
, we use an inductive
definition to keep track of the syntactic structure, in which semantic information is
also maintained. In fact, we need three mutually inductive judgments very similar to typing judgments.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{c}}^{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
stores the syntactic information and the semantic
information of
T
and all its sub-structures.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
stores the syntactic information and the semantic
information of
t
and all its sub-structures.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{i}^{\textsc{d}}\delta:\Delta
stores the syntactic information and the semantic
information of all terms in
\delta
.

We restrict the parameter layer
i\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}
. These definitions will make use of
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\delta:\Delta
.

Their definitions are designed to imply semantic information:

Lemma 7.27.

•

If
