Lemma 7.84.

\frac{L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\Vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}s:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}t[s/x]\approx(\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~s:T[s/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

Proof.

Here
i\in\{\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}
. We obtain
L\Vdash\Psi
,
L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}\Gamma
, and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}T[s/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
following similar lines as the previous lemma.

From the local substitution lemma, we also have
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}t[s/x]:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
so we
are one reduction step away, which can be concluded by the reduction expansion lemma. ∎

Lemma 7.85.

\frac{L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\Vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).(t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~x\approx t:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}}

Proof.

Here
i\in\{\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}
. We assume
\phi\approx\phi^{\prime}
,
\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}
and
\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}
, and finally
s\approx s^{\prime}
. Then see

\displaystyle~(\lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).(t[\phi][\sigma][\delta]:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~x:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~s

\displaystyle\rightsquigarrow

\displaystyle~{(t[\phi][\sigma][\delta]:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~x}[s/x]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{(t[\phi][\sigma][\delta]:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~s}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle~{(t[\phi^{\prime}][\sigma^{\prime}][\delta^{\prime}]:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~s^{\prime}}

Therefore, we obtain the goal by weak head expansion of logical relations.
∎

Lemma 7.86.

If

•

(k,i)\in\{(\textsc{d},\textsc{d}),(\textsc{m},\textsc{d}),(\textsc{m},\textsc{m})\}
,

•

L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\Vdash_{i}M\approx M^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}s_{1}\approx s_{3}:M[\textsf{zero}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

\mathcal{B}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$},y:M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\Vdash_{i}s_{2}\approx s_{4}:M[\textsf{succ}~x/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L^{\prime}\vdash\phi\approx\phi^{\prime}:L
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Psi[\phi]
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{k}^{\Uparrow(i)}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma[\phi][\sigma]
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{k}t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\textsf{zero}$}
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{k}t^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\textsf{zero}$}
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{k}w\simeq w^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\textsf{zero}$}
,

•

\mathcal{A}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{k}w\simeq w^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}
,

•

t_{1}={\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M[\phi][\sigma][\delta,x/x])~(s_{1}[\phi][\sigma][\delta])~(x,y.s_{2}[\phi][\sigma][\delta,x/x,y/y])~t}
,

•

t_{2}={\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}][\sigma^{\prime}][\delta^{\prime},x/x])~(s_{3}[\phi^{\prime}][\sigma^{\prime}][\delta^{\prime}])~(x,y.s_{4}[\phi^{\prime}][\sigma^{\prime}][\delta^{\prime},x/x,y/y])~t^{\prime}}

then

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{k}^{\Uparrow(i)}t_{1}\approx t_{2}:M[\phi][\sigma][\delta,t/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\phi]$}

Proof.

We do induction on
\mathcal{A}
.

•

If
w=w^{\prime}=\textsf{zero}
, then we hit the base case. In this case, we use
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}s_{1}\approx s_{3}:M[\textsf{zero}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and reduction expansion to almost obtain the goal. The only missing piece is to prove

M[\phi][\sigma][\delta,t/x]\approx M[\phi][\sigma][\delta,\textsf{zero}/x]

This holds from symmetry and the fact that
t\approx\textsf{zero}
, so that we can extend

\delta\approx\delta
.

•

If
w=\textsf{succ}~s
and
w^{\prime}=\textsf{succ}~s^{\prime}
, then we apply IH and also
\mathcal{B}
to obtain
the relation between recursive calls for
s
and
s^{\prime}
. We perform a similar analysis to handle the types.

•

If
w=\textsc{v}
and
w=\textsc{v}^{\prime}
for some neutrals, then we relate them using the
reflexivity of neutral.

∎

Lemma 7.87.

\frac{L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\Vdash_{i}M\approx M^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}s_{1}\approx s_{3}:M[\textsf{zero}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$},y:M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\Vdash_{i}s_{2}\approx s_{4}:M[\textsf{succ}~x/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad\mathcal{A}::L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\textsf{zero}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s_{1}~(x,y.s_{2})~t\approx\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l^{\prime}}~(x.M^{\prime})~s_{3}~(x,y.s_{4})~t^{\prime}:M[t/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

Proof.

Instantiate
\mathcal{A}
and use the previous lemma for
i\in\{\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}
. Notice that we know
t
and
t^{\prime}
are related by
Nat
which must reduce to itself
so we can supply all premises required by the previous lemma. When
i=\textsc{c}
, we reuse the proof when
i=\textsc{d}
.
∎

Lemma 7.88.

\frac{L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\Vdash_{i}M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}s:M[\textsf{zero}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$},y:M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\Vdash_{i}s^{\prime}:M[\textsf{succ}~x/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}s\approx\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~\textsf{zero}:M[\textsf{zero}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\Vdash_{i}M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}s:M[\textsf{zero}/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$},y:M\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\Vdash_{i}s^{\prime}:M[\textsf{succ}~x/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}t:\texttt{Nat}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\textsf{zero}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}s^{\prime}[t/x,\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~t/y]\approx\textsf{elim}_{\texttt{Nat}}^{l}~(x.M)~s~(x,y.s^{\prime})~(\textsf{succ}~t):M[\textsf{succ}~t/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

Proof.

We use reduction expansion, semantic local substitution lemma and the previous lemma. ∎

Lemma 7.89.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\Uparrow(i)}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

Proof.

Use irrelevance when
i\in\{\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}
. When
i\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}
, we do a case analysis on the semantics of related terms. Notice that all rules for terms in
Sec.

7.4
contain a type relation. We apply transitivity of related types and irrelevance. ∎

7.9.
More Semantic Rules

In the previous section, we have considered all possible (non-trivial) rules for all
layers. Among these rules, we have looked at the rules for global variables and see how
layering restriction enables code running in the semantics. In this section, we will finish the proof by considering rules that are available at
layer
m
. This will make our proofs in some sense simpler; there is only one layer to
consider. On the other hand, we will look into another important feature, recursors for code,
and how it is semantically justified.

Lemma 7.90.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\Vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\approx\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}

Proof.

Assuming
L^{\prime}\vdash\phi\approx\phi^{\prime}:L
,

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Psi[\phi]
and

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma[\phi][\sigma]
, the
focuses are

•

\mathcal{A}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta[\phi][\sigma]\approx\Delta^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}][\sigma^{\prime}]
,

•

\mathcal{B}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta[\phi][\sigma]\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T[\phi][\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\phi]$}
and

•

\mathcal{C}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Delta^{\prime}[\phi][\sigma]\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T^{\prime}[\phi][\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\phi]$}
.

\mathcal{A}
is simple as we only need to convert weakenings to substitutions. Then we can use
L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
.
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{C}
are symmetric so we only focus on
\mathcal{C}
which is slightly more
complex. We similarly want to convert weakenings to substitutions so that we can apply

L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\Vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
to obtain the goal. The local contexts are mismatched, though it is not a problem. The local contexts are used when further assuming related local substitutions, and
by
L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
, we can use irrelevance to swap the local contexts.
∎

Lemma 7.91.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\approx\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}

Proof.

Similar to above but simpler.
∎

Lemma 7.92.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\Vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\texttt{box}\ t\approx\texttt{box}\ t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}

Proof.

Assuming
L^{\prime}\vdash\phi\approx\phi^{\prime}:L
,

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Psi[\phi]
and

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma[\phi][\sigma]
, then
the goal requires

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{c}}^{\textsc{d}}t[\phi][\sigma]:T[\phi][\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\phi]$}

Notice that the effect of local substitutions is irrelevant anymore. This goal can be instantiate by
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\Vdash_{\textsc{c}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
by passing in
the same universe and global substitutions, and the identity local substitution. ∎

Lemma 7.93.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\Vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\texttt{box}\ T\approx\texttt{box}\ T:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}}

Proof.

Similar to above but simpler.
∎

Lemma 7.94.

\frac{L\vdash l_{1}\approx l_{3}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l_{2}\approx l_{4}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}M\approx M^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,u:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$});\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{1}\approx t_{2}:M[\texttt{box}\ u^{\textsf{id}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\texttt{letbox}^{l_{1}}_{x_{t}.M}~u\leftarrow(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}))~\textsf{in}~t_{1}\approx\texttt{letbox}^{l_{3}}_{x_{T}.M^{\prime}}~u\leftarrow(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{4}$}))~\textsf{in}~t_{2}:M[t/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}

Proof.

Assuming
L^{\prime}\vdash\phi\approx\phi^{\prime}:L
,

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Psi[\phi]
and

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma[\phi][\sigma]
, then
the goal requires

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}s_{1}\approx s_{2}:M[\phi][\sigma][\delta,t[\phi][\sigma][\delta]/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}[\phi]$}

where
s_{1}={\texttt{letbox}^{l_{1}}_{x_{t}.M}~u\leftarrow(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}))~\textsf{in}~t_{1}[\phi][\sigma][\delta]}

and

s_{2}={\texttt{letbox}^{l_{3}}_{x_{T}.M^{\prime}}~u\leftarrow(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{4}$}))~\textsf{in}~t_{2}[\phi^{\prime}][\sigma^{\prime}][\delta^{\prime}]}
.
By instantiating

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
, we have

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}t[\phi][\sigma][\delta]\approx{t^{\prime}}[\phi^{\prime}][\sigma^{\prime}][\delta^{\prime}]:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})[\phi][\sigma][\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}

Unfolding it, there are two possibilities for this relation.

Case

We know for some
t^{\prime\prime}
,

–

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{k}t[\phi][\sigma][\delta]\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})[\phi][\sigma][\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
,

–

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{k}t[\phi^{\prime}][\sigma^{\prime}][\delta^{\prime}]\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})[\phi][\sigma][\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
,
and

–

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta[\phi][\sigma]\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{c}}^{\textsc{d}}t^{\prime\prime}:T[\phi][\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}

We observe that

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}\sigma,t^{\prime\prime}/u\approx\sigma^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}/u:\Psi[\phi],u:(\Delta[\phi][\sigma]\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T[\phi][\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$})

Giving it to
L\;|\;\Psi,u:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$});\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{1}\approx t_{2}:M[\texttt{box}\ u^{\textsf{id}}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}
, we obtain

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}t_{1}[\phi][\sigma,t^{\prime\prime}/u][\delta]\approx{t_{2}}[\phi^{\prime}][\sigma^{\prime},t^{\prime\prime}/u][\delta^{\prime}]:M[\phi][\sigma][\delta,\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime\prime}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}[\phi]$}

By reduction expansion, we have

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}s_{1}\approx s_{2}:M[\phi][\sigma][\delta,\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime\prime}/x_{t}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}[\phi]$}

which is almost the goal. To tame the goal, we observe that

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}t[\phi][\sigma][\delta]\approx\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})[\phi][\sigma][\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}

which further allows us to conclude

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}\delta,t[\phi][\sigma][\delta]/x_{t}\approx\delta^{\prime},\texttt{box}\ t^{\prime\prime}/x_{t}:(\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$})[\phi][\sigma]

Apply
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}M\approx M^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}

gives us the goal using irrelevance and weak head expansion.

Case

We know for some
\nu
and
\nu^{\prime}
,

–

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{k}t[\phi][\sigma][\delta]\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\nu:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})[\phi][\sigma][\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
,

–

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{k}t[\phi^{\prime}][\sigma^{\prime}][\delta^{\prime}]\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\nu^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})[\phi][\sigma][\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
,
and

–

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})[\phi][\sigma][\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}

The idea here is to use the law of congruence for neutrals to establish a generic
equivalence between neutrals, and then we use reflexivity for neutrals to relate
two neutrals using the logical relations.

The process requires us to provide

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime},x_{T}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})[\phi][\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}\delta,x_{T}/x_{T}\approx\delta^{\prime},x_{T}/x_{T}:(\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$})[\phi][\sigma]

This is immediate. We also need

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi,u:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})[\phi][\sigma]\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}\sigma,u^{\textsf{id}}/u\approx\sigma^{\prime},u^{\textsf{id}}/u:(\Psi,u:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}))[\phi]

In this case, we should prove

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi,u:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})[\phi][\sigma];\Delta[\phi][\sigma]\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}u^{\textsf{id}}:T[\phi][\sigma]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}

This turns out to have been checked by the lemma of reflexive global weakening in

Sec.

7.4
. Finally,
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma[\phi][\sigma]

is weakened before applying.

∎

Lemma 7.95.

\frac{L\vdash l_{1}\approx l_{3}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l_{2}\approx l_{4}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x_{T}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}M\approx M^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$});\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t_{1}\approx t_{2}:M[\texttt{box}\ U^{\textsf{id}}/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\texttt{letbox}^{l_{1}}_{x_{T}.M}~U\leftarrow(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}))~\textsf{in}~t_{1}\approx\texttt{letbox}^{l_{3}}_{x_{T}.M^{\prime}}~U\leftarrow(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{4}$}))~\textsf{in}~t_{2}:M[t/x_{T}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}

Proof.

Similar to above but simpler.
∎

Next, we consider the semantics for the recursive principles. The following lemma needs to be mutually proved.

Lemma 7.96.

If

•

S_{A}
,

•

L^{\prime}\vdash\phi\approx\phi^{\prime}:L
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Psi[\phi]
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma[\phi][\sigma]
,

•

L^{\prime}\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta_{1}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\texttt{box}\ T_{1}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta_{1}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\texttt{box}\ T_{1}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
,

•

\mathcal{A}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{c}}^{\textsc{d}}T_{1}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

t_{1}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1}[\phi],l_{2}[\phi]}~{(\overrightarrow{M}[\phi][\sigma][\delta])}~(\overrightarrow{b}[\phi][\sigma][\delta])~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))
, and

•

t_{2}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1}[\phi],l_{2}[\phi]}~{(\overrightarrow{M}^{\prime}[\phi][\sigma][\delta])}~(\overrightarrow{b}^{\prime}[\phi][\sigma][\delta])~(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))
,

then

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}t_{1}\approx t_{2}:M[l/\ell,\Delta/g,t/x_{T}][\phi][\sigma][\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}[\phi]$}

Lemma 7.97.

If

•

S_{A}
,

•

L^{\prime}\vdash\phi\approx\phi^{\prime}:L
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Psi[\phi]
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma[\phi][\sigma]
,

•

L^{\prime}\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta_{1}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\texttt{box}\ t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
,

•

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta_{1}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}\texttt{box}\ t_{1}:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
,

•

\mathcal{B}::L^{\prime}\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{c}}^{\textsc{d}}t_{1}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

•

t_{1}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1}[\phi],l_{2}[\phi]}~{(\overrightarrow{M}[\phi][\sigma][\delta])}~(\overrightarrow{b}[\phi][\sigma][\delta])~(t:\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))
, and

•

t_{2}=\textsf{elim}^{l_{1}[\phi],l_{2}[\phi]}~{(\overrightarrow{M}^{\prime}[\phi][\sigma][\delta])}~(\overrightarrow{b}^{\prime}[\phi][\sigma][\delta])~(t^{\prime}:\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}))
,

then

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta_{1}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}t_{1}\approx t_{2}:M^{\prime}[l/\ell,\Delta/g,T/U_{T},t/x_{t}][\phi][\sigma][\delta]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}[\phi]$}

where
S_{A}
is the set containing all semantic judgments for motives and branches in

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma
.

Proof.

The idea is to do a mutual induction on
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
. Recall that they are mutually defined structures as shown in

Sec.

7.4
. Let us pick two cases to discuss:

Case

\frac{u:(\Delta_{2}\vdash_{i^{\prime}}T_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime\prime}$})\in\Phi\quad i^{\prime}\in\{\textsc{v},\textsc{c}\}\quad L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{c}}^{\textsc{d}}\delta_{2}:\Delta_{2}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T_{2}[\delta_{2}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}u^{\delta_{2}}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{c}}^{\textsc{d}}u^{\delta_{2}}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

In this case, we must block the evaluation. The idea follows closely to the neutral case for
letbox
and we should apply
the law of neutral recursion on code. In this case, we see that
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\simeq\Delta^{\prime}
,

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
. Then we have to show that the motives and the branches are related by generic
equivalence. The idea is to extend all substitutions if necessary. We have shown that all substitutions can be extended by identities in the
semantics (and universe substitutions are the same in both syntax and
semantics).

Case

\frac{L^{\prime}\vdash l_{3}:\textsf{Level}\quad L^{\prime}\vdash l_{4}:\textsf{Level}\quad L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{c}}^{\textsc{d}}S_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{3}$}\quad L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:{S_{2}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{3}$}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{c}}^{\textsc{d}}t_{2}:T_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{4}$}\quad L^{\prime}\vdash l\approx l_{3}\sqcup l_{4}:\textsf{Level}\quad L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx\Pi^{l_{3},l_{4}}(x:S_{2}).T_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}\lambda^{l_{3},l_{4}}(x:S_{2}).t_{2}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{c}}^{\textsc{d}}\lambda^{l_{3},l_{4}}(x:S_{2}).t_{2}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

In this case, we should go down and recurse on
S_{2}
and
t_{2}
. We then use the semantic rule for
t_{\lambda}
to substitute in the results of
the recursive calls for
S_{2}
and
t_{2}
. We also obtain
L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:{S_{2}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{3}$}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
. Therefore we have everything we need to use the semantic rule for
t_{\lambda}
. The only missing piece is that the conclusion requires relation between
T
and

T^{\prime}
. Meanwhile,
t_{\lambda}
only gives us
{\Pi^{l_{3},l_{4}}(x:S_{2}).T_{2}}
. The solution lies in

\displaystyle L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

\displaystyle L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\geq\textsc{d}}^{\textsc{d}}T\approx\Pi^{l_{3},l_{4}}(x:S_{2}).T_{2}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

Thus these three types are related. Since
{\Pi^{l_{3},l_{4}}(x:S_{2}).T_{2}}
is already in normal form, so we know both

T^{\prime}
and
T^{\prime}
must reduce to it. Together with the
\beta
rule when hitting the
\lambda
case, we use weak head
expansion to obtain the desired goal.

∎

These two lemmas in the semantics give the recursions on code of types and terms and
actual do the recursions. For the semantic rules for the recursors, we first use these lemmas to prove the
congruence rules. We are almost done wit the congruence rules except that we have to handle the neutral
cases. This is virtually identical to the global variable cases, where we use the law of
neutral recursion of code to relate neutral terms and use reflexivity for neutrals to
establish the logical relations. The rest are the
\beta
rules. They are even simpler due to access to the congruence rules. Then we use reduction expansion to achieve the goals.

What are left now are the meta-functions including universe-polymorphic functions. Fist the meta-functions for local contexts and types are very similar.

Lemma 7.98.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\vdash l_{1}\approx l_{3}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l_{2}\approx l_{4}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}))\Rightarrow^{l_{2}}T\approx(U:(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{3}$}))\Rightarrow^{l_{4}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}

Proof.

Assuming
L^{\prime}\vdash\phi\approx\phi^{\prime}:L
,

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Psi[\phi]
and

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma[\phi][\sigma]
, then
we should prove

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}))\Rightarrow^{l_{2}}T[\phi][\sigma][\delta]\approx(U:(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{3}$}))\Rightarrow^{l_{4}}T^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}][\sigma^{\prime}][\delta^{\prime}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}[\phi]$}

Most premises are simple. The tricky part is to show
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2}
, which are
symmetric. Knowing
L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
, it is sufficient to only prove one of them. Using
L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
, it is easy to see that
both
\mathcal{E}_{1}
and
E_{2}
hold, by extending
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 7.99.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\approx(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

Proof.

Similar to above but simpler.
∎

Lemma 7.100.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$});\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}\quad L\vdash l_{1}\approx l_{3}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l_{2}\approx l_{4}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Lambda^{l_{1},l_{2}}_{p}~{U}.t\approx\Lambda^{l_{3},l_{4}}_{p}~{U}.t^{\prime}:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}))\Rightarrow^{l_{2}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}

Proof.

In this proof, we are asked to prove two symmetric proof which is essentially to
show that the results of applying
{\Lambda^{l_{1},l_{2}}_{p}~{U}.t}
and

{\Lambda^{l_{3},l_{4}}_{p}~{U}.t^{\prime}}
are related. This is immediate by extending related global substitutions and applying them to

L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$});\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}
. ∎

Lemma 7.101.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\Vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t~\$_{p}~T\approx t^{\prime}~\$_{p}~T^{\prime}:T^{\prime\prime}[T/U]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

Proof.

We obtain the goal quite easily by using the semantics of related terms of type

(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 7.102.

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}\quad L\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\Vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[T/U]\approx(\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.t)~\$_{p}~T:T^{\prime}[T/U]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Lambda^{l,l^{\prime}}_{p}~{U}.(t~\$_{p}~U^{\textsf{id}})\approx t:(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}

Proof.

These two rules are also very simple to prove and follow similar lines to those of

\Pi
types. For the
\beta
rule, we use reduction expansion. For the
\eta
rule, we see it by
noticing applying related global substitutions. ∎

The corresponding introduction, elimination,
\beta
and
\eta
rules for
meta-functions for local contexts are proved similarly but just simpler.

Now the only last piece is universe-polymorphic functions.

Lemma 7.103.

\frac{L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad|\overrightarrow{\ell}|>0\quad L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T\approx\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\omega$}}

Proof.

Assuming
L^{\prime}\vdash\phi\approx\phi^{\prime}:L
,

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}\sigma\approx\sigma^{\prime}:\Psi[\phi]
and

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}\delta\approx\delta^{\prime}:\Gamma[\phi][\sigma]
, then
we should prove

L^{\prime}\;|\;\Phi;\Delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\raisebox{0.01pt}{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}\hskip 1.49994pt{\rule{0.45pt}{6.88889pt}}{\hskip 1.00006pt\vDash}}_{\textsc{m}}^{\textsc{m}}\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T[\phi][\sigma][\delta]\approx\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime}[\phi^{\prime}][\sigma^{\prime}][\delta^{\prime}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\omega$}

Looking at the semantics of universe-polymorphic functions, we see that

L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
has already provided the goal. It is important to see that
L^{\prime}\vdash l[\phi,\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]:\textsf{Level}
if all
universe levels in
\overrightarrow{l}
are well-formed so it is strictly smaller than

\omega
. Therefore, we still have a well-founded semantics.
∎

Lemma 7.104.

\frac{L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad|\overrightarrow{\ell}|>0\quad L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\vdash l\approx l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Lambda^{l}~{\overrightarrow{\ell}}.t\approx\Lambda^{l^{\prime}}~{\overrightarrow{\ell}}.t^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\omega$}}\par\frac{L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t\approx t^{\prime}:\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\omega$}\quad|\overrightarrow{\ell}|=|\overrightarrow{l}|=|\overrightarrow{l}^{\prime}|>0\quad\forall 0\leq n<|\overrightarrow{l}|~.~L\vdash\overrightarrow{l}(n)\approx\overrightarrow{l}^{\prime}(n):\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t~\$~\overrightarrow{l}\approx t^{\prime}~\$~\overrightarrow{l}^{\prime}:T[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Gamma\quad L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\vdash l:\textsf{Level}\quad|\overrightarrow{\ell}|=|\overrightarrow{l}|>0\quad\forall l^{\prime}\in\overrightarrow{l}~.~L\vdash l^{\prime}:\textsf{Level}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]\approx(\Lambda^{l}~{\overrightarrow{\ell}}.t)~\$~\overrightarrow{l}:T[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l[\overrightarrow{l}/\overrightarrow{\ell}]$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\omega$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\Vdash_{\textsc{m}}\Lambda^{l}~{\overrightarrow{\ell}}.(t~\$~\overrightarrow{\ell})\approx t:\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\omega$}}

Proof.

All these rules are relatively simple. The congruence rule for introduction can be proved by following the definition of
related terms of type
{\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T}
.

The congruence rule for elimination can be proved by using the definition of related
terms of type
{\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T}
.

The
\beta
rule can be derived from using reduction expansion.

The
\eta
rule can be given after applying arbitrary equivalent universe
substitutions. ∎

At this point, we have proved all semantic rules and thus the fundamental theorems
hold.

8.
Consequences and Decidability of Convertibility

In the previous section, we have established the fundamental theorems, and using the
escape lemma, we see that all syntactic equivalent types and terms are also
semantically related by the Kripke logical relations. In this section, we will instantiate the generic equivalence so that we can obtain
desired properties that are difficult to prove syntactically.

8.1.
First Instantiation: Syntactic Equivalence

First we instantiate the generic equivalence with syntactic equivalences of types and
terms. The laws are all easily instantiated by the corresponding equivalence rules. We also see that the derived equivalences between local contexts and local
substitutions are equivalent to the corresponding syntactic equivalences. From the fundamental theorems and the escape lemma, we are able to derive the
following lemmas.

Lemma 8.1 (Injectivity of Type Constructors).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\approx\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S^{\prime}).T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}
and

i\in\{\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S\approx S^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
.

•

If

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:s).t\approx\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:s^{\prime}).t^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{l\sqcup l^{\prime}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\textsf{succ}~(l\sqcup l^{\prime})$}
and
i\in\{\textsc{d},\textsc{m}\}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}s\approx s^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{l}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l$}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:\texttt{El}^{l}~s\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:\texttt{Ty}_{l^{\prime}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+l^{\prime}$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\approx\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\square(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\approx\square(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{c}}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$})\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~0$}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T\approx(g:\textsf{Ctx})\Rightarrow^{l}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then

L\;|\;\Psi,g:\textsf{Ctx};\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}(U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T\approx(U:(\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}))\Rightarrow^{l^{\prime}}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\Delta\approx\Delta^{\prime}
and

L\;|\;\Psi,U:(\Delta\vdash_{\textsc{d}}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$});\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T\approx\overrightarrow{\ell}\Rightarrow^{l}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\omega$}
, then

L,\overrightarrow{\ell}\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{\textsc{m}}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Proof.

By fundamental theorems, we pass in all identity substitutions and then we can
extract this fact from the logical relations of types.
∎

We follow
Hu
et al. (2023)
and give us the consistency proof of
DeLaM
.

Lemma 8.2 (Consistency).

There is no term
t
that satisfies this typing judgment:

\ell\;|\;\cdot;\cdot\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:\Pi^{1+\ell,\ell}(x:\texttt{Ty}_{\ell}).x\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+\ell$}

That is, there is not a generic way to construct a term of an arbitrary type.

Proof.

The lemma effectively asks to reject the following derivation after applying the
fundamental theorems:

\ell\;|\;\cdot;x:\texttt{Ty}_{\ell}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+\ell$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}t:x\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$}

Now by the logical relations of the neutral type
x
, we know that
t
must also be
neutral, so we now move on to rejecting

\ell\;|\;\cdot;x:\texttt{Ty}_{\ell}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~1+\ell$}\vdash_{\textsc{m}}\textsc{v}:x\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~\ell$}

Then we do induction on
v
. It is impossible to do any operation on
v
but to refer to
x
ultimately, but then

x
has type
\texttt{Ty}_{\ell}
which cannot be equivalent to
x
, as they both have reached
normal forms and it is not possible to relate any two distinguished normal forms in
any case by the logical relations. ∎

8.2.
Second Instantiation: Convertibility Checking

In this section, we perform our second instantiation by specifying the generic
equivalence to be the convertibility checking judgments. This second instantiation is not very immediate for some laws for the generic
equivalence, so we must do some verification, using the results from the first
instantiation.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}V\sim V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
is
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}V\longleftrightarrow V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\simeq T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
is
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
is
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\simeq t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
is
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Most laws are quite straightforward, except the conversion laws and the PER laws. Let us consider the conversion laws first. We must show that the convertibility checking algorithm is invariant under contexts
and types. Intuitively, this should be true, but it is not very easy to prove, until we obtain
the injectivity lemma after the first instantiation.

Lemma 8.3 (Conversion).

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and

L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}W\Longleftrightarrow W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and

L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}W\Longleftrightarrow W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}V\longleftrightarrow V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and

L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}V\longleftrightarrow V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \Delta
and
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
, then
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \Delta
.

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
,
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma

and
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\approx T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then

L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w\Longleftrightarrow w^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
,
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma

and
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}W^{\prime}\approx W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}w\approx w^{\prime}:W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
;

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and

L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\nu\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\approx T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
for some
T^{\prime}
.

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and

L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\nu\approx\nu^{\prime}:W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}W^{\prime}\approx W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
for some
W^{\prime}
.

•

if
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
,

L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Gamma^{\prime}\approx\Gamma
and
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta^{\prime}\approx\Delta
, then

L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}\delta\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We do induction on all derivations. Most cases are immediate. When we get under binders, we need to extend the context equivalences, in which case
we should use the soundness lemma to obtain the well-formedness of the type that
needs to be extended to the contexts.

We consider a few cases.

Case

\frac{T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w\Longleftrightarrow w^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

The most important thing to notice is that by
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\approx T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

and
T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W
, we know that
T^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W^{\prime}
for some
W^{\prime}
. Therefore we can prove
this case using IH. Other premises are satisfied by syntactic context equivalence lemmas.

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}(w:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~x\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ (w^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w\Longleftrightarrow w^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}}

In this case, we know
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}W^{\prime}\approx\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}
. By the fundamental theorems, we know that
W^{\prime}
can only reduce to some
\Pi

type. Since
W^{\prime}
is already normal, it is only possible for
W^{\prime}
to be some
\Pi

type. Say
W^{\prime}={\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S^{\prime}).T^{\prime}}
. Then by injectivity, we know
S\approx S^{\prime}
and
T\approx T^{\prime}
. We obtain our goal by extending the local contexts.

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\Longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:V\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

In this case, the type equivalence is irrelevant. When hitting neutral types, we simply ignore the type
W
inferred by

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

In this case, we simply apply IH. Then we know

T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W\text{ and }T^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W^{\prime}

Our goal is to show
W\approx W^{\prime}
. But this is immediate from the determinacy lemma of multi-step reduction and the
fundamental theorems.

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\quad x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\in\Gamma}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}x\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

The goal is given by the equivalence between
\Gamma
and
\Delta
.

Case

\frac{L\vdash l_{1}\approx l_{3}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\vdash l_{2}\approx l_{4}:\textsf{Level}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ S^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S^{\prime\prime}).T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}\sqcup l_{2}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}s\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ s^{\prime}:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}(\nu:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S).T)~s\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ (\nu^{\prime}:\Pi^{l_{3},l_{4}}(x:S^{\prime}).T^{\prime})~s^{\prime}:T[s/x]\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{2}$}}

By IH, we know from

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S^{\prime\prime}).T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}\sqcup l_{2}$}
that there
must be
W^{\prime}
, so that

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}W^{\prime}\approx\Pi^{l_{1},l_{2}}(x:S^{\prime\prime}).T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l_{1}\sqcup l_{2}$}

By the fundamental theorems, we know
W^{\prime}
must be some
\Pi
types. The return type is fixed so we do not have to do anything.

∎

From the conversion lemma, we see that both
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
return some equivalent types because they are in fact
inference steps. Therefore, it makes sense when we instantiate
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, we hide a
syntactic equivalence judgment inside.

For the PER laws, we see that the difficulties primarily come from the convertibility
checking of neutrals, because again they are inference steps. Moreover, due to their left-biased design, in general it is not true that the inferred
types can be replaced by their equivalence. However, since we are hiding an equivalence judgment in
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu\sim\nu^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

during instantiation, following the same principle as the conversion lemma, we are
able to erase the effect of the left bias and establish the PER laws.

The instantiation immediately gives us the convertibility lemma once we apply the
escape lemma:

Theorem 8.4 (Convertibility).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\approx T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\approx t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
.

In particular, we see that the convertibility checking algorithm is both sound and
complete w.r.t. the syntactic equivalence judgments. The decidability of type checking requires the following lemma by attempting to relate
two reflexively convertible types or terms.

Lemma 8.5 (Decidability).

•

if
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then whether

L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
is decidable.

•

if
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}W\Longleftrightarrow W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}W^{\prime}\Longleftrightarrow W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then whether

L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}W\Longleftrightarrow W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
is decidable.

•

if
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}V\longleftrightarrow V\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}V^{\prime}\longleftrightarrow V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then whether

L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}V\longleftrightarrow V^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
is decidable.

•

if
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \Gamma
,
L\;|\;\Psi\vdash_{i}\Delta\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \Delta
and

L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
, then whether
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Gamma\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \Delta
is decidable.

•

if
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then whether

L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
is decidable.

•

if
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}w\Longleftrightarrow w:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w^{\prime}\Longleftrightarrow w^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and
L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then whether

L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}w\approx w^{\prime}:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
is decidable.

•

if
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\nu\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu^{\prime}\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and

L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then whether

L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\nu\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
for some
T^{\prime\prime}
is decidable.

•

if
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu^{\prime}\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and

L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Delta\approx\Gamma
, then whether

L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\nu\approx\nu^{\prime}:W^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
is decidable.

•

if
L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}\delta\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \delta:\Delta
,

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\delta^{\prime}\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta
,
L\vdash\Phi\approx\Psi
and

L\;|\;\Phi\vdash_{i}\Gamma^{\prime}\approx\Gamma
, then whether

L\;|\;\Phi;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{i}\delta\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ \delta^{\prime}:\Delta
is decidable.

Proof.

We do a mutual induction on the first derivations and then invert the second ones. We can reject most cases when they have different root derivations. We consider a few cases.

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}W\Longleftrightarrow W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}W^{\prime}\Longleftrightarrow W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

In this case, we apply IH to decide whether
W
and
W^{\prime}
are convertible.

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}S\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\Longleftrightarrow\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S^{\prime}\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ S^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S^{\prime}).T^{\prime}\Longleftrightarrow\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S^{\prime}).T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}}

We can decide whether
S
and
S^{\prime}
are convertible by IH. When we decide
T
and
T^{\prime}
, we must extend the equivalent local contexts.

Case

\frac{T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W\quad L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}t\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad\quad L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}w\Longleftrightarrow w:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{T^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W^{\prime}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}w^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w^{\prime}\Longleftrightarrow w^{\prime}:W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

By fundamental theorems, we know
W\approx W^{\prime}
. Then by IH, we can decide whether
w
and
w^{\prime}
are convertible.

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}w:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}(w:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~x\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ (w:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}w\Longleftrightarrow w:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}\quad L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma,x:S\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\vdash_{i}(w^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~x\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ (w^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T)~x:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l^{\prime}$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}w^{\prime}\Longleftrightarrow w^{\prime}:\Pi^{l,l^{\prime}}(x:S).T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l\sqcup l^{\prime}$}}

Again, it is quite immediate by IH.

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\nu\Longleftrightarrow\nu:V\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu^{\prime}\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu^{\prime}\Longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:V\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

By IH, we know
L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:W^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
for some
W^{\prime\prime}
.

Case

\frac{L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\nu\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad T\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W}{L\;|\;\Phi;\Delta\vdash_{i}\nu\longleftrightarrow\nu:W\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}\par\frac{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu^{\prime}\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}\quad T^{\prime}\rightsquigarrow^{\ast}W^{\prime}}{L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu^{\prime}\longleftrightarrow\nu^{\prime}:W^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}}

By IH, we have
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}\nu^{\prime}\ \hat{\longleftrightarrow}\ \nu^{\prime}:T^{\prime\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
for some
T^{\prime\prime}
.
T^{\prime\prime}
will reduce to some normal form by the fundamental theorems.

∎

Theorem 8.6 (Decidability of Convertibility).

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then whether
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}T\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ T^{\prime}\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}

is decidable.

•

If
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
and
L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
, then whether

L\;|\;\Psi;\Gamma\vdash_{i}t\ \hat{\Longleftrightarrow}\ t^{\prime}:T\scalebox{0.75}{\color[rgb]{0.5,0.5,0.5}$\,@~l$}
is decidable.

Proof.

First we use the fundamental theorems from the second instantiation to show that
both types (or terms, resp.) are reflexively convertible. Then we use the decidability lemma above. ∎

At this point, we have justified the decidability of convertibility checking of

DeLaM
and therefore conclude our investigations.
